Forward production of a Drell-Yan pair and a jet at small x x at next-to-leading order
Pieter Taels

Abstract
We perform the analytical next-to-leading order calculation of the process p + A → γ ∗ + jet + X p+A\to\gamma^{*}+\mathrm{jet}+X , at forward rapidities and low x x . These kinematics justify a hybrid approach, where a quark from the ‘projectile’ proton scatters off the gluon distribution of the ‘target’, which can be a nucleus or a highly boosted proton. By using the Color Glass Condensate effective theory approach, this gluon distribution is allowed to be so dense that the quark undergoes multiple scattering. Moreover, large high-energy logarithms in the ratio of the hard scale and the center-of-mass energy are resummed by the Balitsky, Kovchegov, Jalilian-Marian, Iancu, McLerran, Weigert, Leonidov, Kovner or BK-JIMWLK evolution equations. We demonstrate that all ultraviolet divergences encountered in the calculation cancel, while the high-energy divergences are absorbed into BK-JIMWLK. The remaining singularities are collinear in nature and can be either absorbed into the Dokshitzer-Gribov-Lipatov-Altarelli-Parisi evolution of the incoming quark, when they stem from initial-state radiation, or else can be treated by introduction of a jet function in case they are caused by final-state emissions. The resulting cross section is completely finite and expressed in function of only a small set of color operators.



1
Introduction

In order to apply Quantum Chromodynamics (QCD) to the analysis of collider experiments with hadrons, the standard approach is to rely on collinear factorization. A hard scale
\mu^{2}
justifies a perturbative treatment of the underlying partonic hard scattering process, while the hadron structure is parameterized by parton distribution functions (PDFs) or fragmentation functions (FFs). Using the Dokshitzer-Gribov-Lipatov-Altarelli-Parisi (DGLAP) equations
Gribov:1972ri
;
Dokshitzer:1977sg
;
Altarelli:1977zs
, the scale dependence of the PDFs and FFs can be perturbatively calculated, resumming large collinear logarithms in the ratio
\mu^{2}/\mu^{2}_{0}
of the hard scale and the hadronic one.

However, collinear factorization is known to break down whenever there is an additional large ratio of scales in the process under consideration. For example, it is implicitly assumed that the center-of-mass energy
\sqrt{s}
is of the same order as the hard scale:
s\sim\mu^{2}
. At high energies or low
x\sim\mu^{2}/s
, this condition can be violated, and it becomes necessary to perform an additional resummation of large high-energy- or rapidity logarithms
\alpha_{s}\ln s/\mu^{2}\sim\alpha_{s}\ln 1/x\gg 1
. A commonly used framework to do so is High-Energy Factorization (HEF)
Catani:1990xk
;
Catani:1990eg
;
Catani:1994sq
, in which the Balitsky-Fadin-Kuraev-Lipatov (BFKL) equations
Kuraev:1977fs
;
Balitsky:1978ic
are used to resum the high-energy logarithms on top of the collinear ones already resummed by DGLAP.

In this paper, we work within another framework applicable at small
x
, known as the Color Glass Condensate (CGC)
McLerran:1993ni
;
McLerran:1993ka
;
McLerran:1994vd
;
Jalilian-Marian:1997qno
;
Jalilian-Marian:1997jhx
;
Jalilian-Marian:1997ubg
;
Kovner:2000pt
;
Weigert:2000gi
;
Iancu:2000hn
;
Iancu:2001ad
;
Ferreiro:2001qy
. The CGC is an effective theory based upon the separation of fast and slow gluon fields in a highly boosted proton or nucleus (a shockwave). Only the former are regarded as quantum fields, while the latter are integrated out and treated as a semiclassical background field with a characteristic scale
Q_{s}
. This scale is known as the saturation scale, because it marks the onset of a regime in which nonlinear gluon recombinations counteract the exponential growth of the gluon density predicted by BFKL
Gribov:1983ivg
. The renormalization group evolution associated with the CGC effective theory leads to the nonlinear Balitsky, Kovchegov, Jalilian-Marian, Iancu, McLerran, Weigert, Leonidov, Kovner or BK-JIMWLK evolution equations
Balitsky:1995ub
;
Balitsky:1998kc
;
Balitsky:1998ya
;
Kovchegov:1999yj
;
Jalilian-Marian:1997qno
;
Jalilian-Marian:1997jhx
;
Jalilian-Marian:1997ubg
;
Kovner:2000pt
;
Weigert:2000gi
;
Iancu:2000hn
;
Iancu:2001ad
;
Ferreiro:2001qy
. In a sense, JIMWLK can be regarded as the generalization to the nonlinear saturation regime of the linear BFKL evolution, to which it reduces in the hard scattering limit
k_{\scriptscriptstyle T}^{2}\gg Q_{s}^{2}
.

Although there have been many hints, conclusive experimental proof of saturation is still lacking, see for instance
Morreale:2021pnn
and references therein. After all, it is an effect hidden in a corner of phase space. Precise theoretical predictions are, therefore, essential, and since the last decade a lot of progress has been made to compute several relevant processes in the saturation regime at next-to-leading order (NLO) accuracy. Nowadays, the impact factors are known for single inclusive hadron production in proton-nucleus collisions
Chirilli:2011km
;
Chirilli:2012jd
, inclusive deep-inelastic scattering (DIS)
Balitsky:2010ze
;
Balitsky:2012bs
;
Beuf:2011xd
;
Beuf:2016wdz
;
Beuf:2017bpd
;
Hanninen:2017ddy
, DIS with massive quarks
Beuf:2021qqa
;
Beuf:2021srj
;
Beuf:2022ndu
, exclusive vector meson production in DIS
Boussarie:2016bkq
;
Mantysaari:2022bsp
;
Mantysaari:2021ryb
;
Mantysaari:2022kdm
, photon plus dijet production in DIS
Roy:2019hwr
, diffractive dijet or dihadron production in DIS
Boussarie:2016ogo
;
Fucilla:2022wcg
, inclusive dijet
Caucal:2021ent
or dihadron
Bergabo:2022tcu
;
Bergabo:2023wed
production in DIS, semi-inclusive DIS
Bergabo:2022zhe
, and inclusive dijet photoproduction
Taels:2022tza
. The next-to-leading logarithmic extension of the JIMWLK equations has been studied in refs.
Balitsky:2013fea
;
Kovner:2013ona
;
Kovner:2014lca
;
Lublinsky:2016meo
. Finally, results for multiparticle production at tree level were obtained in
Ayala:2016lhd
;
Iancu:2018hwa
;
Altinoluk:2018byz
;
Altinoluk:2020qet
;
Iancu:2020mos
;
Iancu:2022gpw
.

In this work, we contribute to the above-mentioned theory effort by presenting the next-to-leading order calculation of the process
p+A\to\gamma^{*}+\mathrm{jet}+X
at forward rapidities and at high energies. The resulting cross section is easily promoted to the inclusive production of a Drell-Yan pair
Drell:1970wh
plus a jet by multiplying with the
\gamma^{*}\to\ell^{-}+\ell^{+}
decay rate, provided the azimuthal angle between the two leptons is integrated out
Berger:2001wr
. At leading order in the CGC, the calculation was first performed in ref.
Gelis:2002fw
and the process further studied in, e.g., ref.
Stasto:2012ru
;
Basso:2015pba
. Within the HEF framework, low-
x
inclusive Drell-Yan plus jet production has been studied in refs.
vanHameren:2015uia
;
Deak:2018obv
.

In the kinematics under consideration, a quark or gluon carrying a longitudinal momentum fraction
x_{p}
from the ‘projectile’ proton, probes the low-
x_{\scriptscriptstyle{A}}
gluon distribution of the ‘target’ proton or nucleus. This motivates us to work in an approximation known as the hybrid or dilute-dense factorization scheme
Dumitru:2005gt
;
Elastic_vs_Inelastic
, in which the partonic structure of the projectile proton can be parameterized with collinear PDFs, while the CGC is applied to the gluon structure of the target.
1
1

1

In the recently appeared work
Altinoluk:2023hfz
, the hybrid approach has been generalized to include transverse-momentum dependence also on the projectile side.
The rationale behind this is that the typical transverse momenta of the target constituents are of the order of the saturation scale
Q_{s}^{2}
, while those of the projectile have approximately the size of the hadronic one
\mu_{0}^{2}
. Therefore, one expects the error corresponding to this approximation to be parametrically
\mu_{0}^{2}/Q_{s}^{2}
, which at very low
x
can indeed become negligible (around
1\%
for
x\sim 10^{-5}
). In spirit, this hybrid scheme is in fact very similar to the dipole picture
Mueller:1989st
;
Nikolaev:1990ja
used in deep-inelastic scattering. In particular, it allows one to formulate the
p+A\to\gamma^{*}+\mathrm{jet}+X
cross section as a convolution of the projectile quark PDF with the perturbative
q\to\gamma^{*}+q(+g)
splitting inside the semiclassical color background field of the target, described by the CGC. In this work, we limit ourselves to the quark channel, and perform the calculation using light-cone perturbation theory (LCPT)
Kogut:1969xa
;
Bjorken:1970ah
;
Brodsky:1997de
. We refer to
Beuf:2016wdz
for a very clear presentation of LCPT in the context of the dipole picture and the CGC.

As in most higher-order calculations, we will encounter different classes of divergences. We will use the standard approach of dimensional regularization
tHooft:1972tcz
to treat ultraviolet (UV) and infrared (IR) singularities. At the present perturbative order, the former only appear in the loop diagrams, and will be shown to cancel in the total virtual NLO contribution. Infrared or, in this case, collinear divergences, appear both in virtual diagrams (more specifically in the asymptotic quark self-energy corrections), and on the cross section level after integrating over the momentum of the radiated gluon. The poles stemming from collinear gluon radiation in the initial state will be shown to cancel with the DGLAP evolution of the incoming quark, while those in the final state will be regulated by a jet definition. Finally, we will regularize high-energy or rapidity divergences with a cutoff method, as is customary in CGC calculations, and demonstrate how they are absorbed in the JIMWLK evolution of the target.

The paper is organized as follows: we start with the derivation of the cross section at leading order in section
2
, which allows us to introduce the conventions of our light-cone perturbation theory approach to the CGC. In section
3
, we list all the one-loop virtual diagrams and their corresponding amplitudes. The cancellation of the ultraviolet divergences which they contain is then treated in section
4
. In the next section,
5
, we present the results of all the real radiative corrections to the cross section. The treatment of the corresponding collinear singularities in the initial and final state is discussed in sections
6
and
7
, respectively. In section
8
, we show how the remaining rapidity singularities can be absorbed into the JIMWLK evolution of the leading-order cross section, after which we present the completely finite total cross section in section
9
. We conclude with a discussion of the applications of our calculation and further research directions. Readers interested in the technicalities of deriving the one-loop amplitudes in our framework are referred to the appendix, section
C
, where the calculation of one virtual diagram is presented in full detail.

2
Leading-order cross section

Figure 1:
The two Feynman graphs for the LO partonic
q+A\to\gamma^{*}+q

process. The highly boosted target
A
: a shockwave, is represented
by the full vertical line.

In this first section, we briefly review the leading-order (LO) calculation
of inclusive forward virtual photon plus jet production (
p+A\to\gamma^{*}+\mathrm{jet}+X
).
Along the way, we will specify the notations and conventions that
we use throughout this work. First and foremost, let us introduce
our convention for light-cone (LC) momenta, namely:

k^{+}=\frac{k_{0}+k_{3}}{\sqrt{2}}\quad\mathrm{and}\quad k^{-}=\frac{k_{0}-k_{3}}{\sqrt{2}}\;,

(1)

where
3
is the direction along the beam line. Transverse momenta
or coordinates are always written in boldface, and directly evaluated
in Euclidean space. The metric is, therefore, given by the product:

k\cdot p=k^{+}p^{-}+k^{-}p^{+}-\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{p}\;.

(2)

In light-cone perturbation theory, all particle momenta obey
the mass-shell condition:
k^{-}=(\mathbf{k}^{2}+m^{2})/2k^{+}
.
For this reason, we can use the compact notation
\vec{k}=(k^{+},\mathbf{k})

for a generic momentum vector.

Figure
1
depicts the two Feynman diagrams corresponding
to the partonic
q+A\to\gamma^{*}+q
process. All fermions are taken
to be massless, and we choose a frame in which the momentum of the
incoming quark is oriented along the positive light-cone direction:

\vec{p}_{0}=(p_{0}^{+},\mathbf{p}_{0}=0)\;.

(3)

As is conventional, the gluon fields are described using the light-cone
gauge
A^{+}=0.
Such a gauge condition cannot, however, be set for
the virtual photon field, which has a virtual mass
M
and is described
by the Proca equation. Taking
\vec{q}=(q^{+},\mathbf{q})
to be
the momentum of the virtual photon, we make the following choice for
its transverse (
\lambda=1,2
) and longitudinal polarization vectors

\epsilon_{\lambda}^{\mu}
resp.
\epsilon_{0}^{\mu}
:

\displaystyle\epsilon_{\lambda}^{\mu}

\displaystyle=\big(0,\frac{\mathbf{q}\cdot\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{\lambda}}{q^{+}},\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{\lambda}\big)\qquad\mathrm{and}\qquad\epsilon_{0}^{\mu}=\big(\frac{q^{+}}{M},\frac{\mathbf{q}^{2}-M^{2}}{2q^{+}M},\frac{\mathbf{q}}{M}\big)\;.

(4)

Furthermore, in what follows we work with linear transverse polarization
vectors
\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{\lambda}^{i}=\delta^{i\lambda}
.

In
D
dimensions, the leading-order partonic cross section is given
by:

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\hat{\sigma}_{\mathrm{LO}}

\displaystyle=\!\frac{1}{2p_{0}^{+}}\frac{\mathrm{d}p_{1}^{+}\mathrm{\mathrm{d}}^{D\!-\!2}\mathbf{p}_{1}\,\theta(p_{1}^{+})}{(2\pi)^{D\!-\!1}2p_{1}^{+}}\frac{\mathrm{d}q^{+}\mathrm{\mathrm{d}}^{D\!-\!2}\mathbf{q}\,\theta(q^{+})}{(2\pi)^{D\!-\!1}2q^{+}}2\pi\delta(p_{0}^{+}\!-\!p_{1}^{+}\!-\!q^{+})\frac{1}{D\!-\!2}\big|\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LO1\!+\!2}}\big|^{2}\;,

(5)

where
\vec{p}_{1}=(p_{1}^{+},\mathbf{p}_{1})
is the momentum of
the outgoing quark, and where
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LO1+2}}
is the sum of the
two amplitudes associated with the diagrams in figure
1
.
From eq. (
5
), the
pp
- or
pA
cross section
is obtained after convolving with the quark PDF and averaging over
the semiclassical gluon fields in the target (an operation we denote
with
\langle...\rangle
):

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\sigma_{\mathrm{LO}}

\displaystyle=\int\frac{\mathrm{d}p_{0}^{+}}{p_{0}^{+}}\frac{p_{0}^{+}}{p_{p}^{+}}f_{q}\big(\frac{p_{0}^{+}}{p_{p}^{+}},\mu^{2}\big)\langle\mathrm{d}\hat{\sigma}_{\mathrm{LO}}\rangle\;,

(6)

\displaystyle=x_{p}f_{q}(x_{p},\mu^{2})\frac{2\pi}{2(p_{0}^{+})^{2}}\frac{\mathrm{d}p_{1}^{+}\mathrm{\mathrm{d}}^{D-2}\mathbf{p}_{1}\,\theta(p_{1}^{+})}{(2\pi)^{D-1}2p_{1}^{+}}\frac{\mathrm{d}q^{+}\mathrm{\mathrm{d}}^{D-2}\mathbf{q}\,\theta(q^{+})}{(2\pi)^{D-1}2q^{+}}

\displaystyle\times\frac{1}{D-2}\big\langle\big|\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LO1}}+\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LO2}}\big|^{2}\big\rangle\Big|_{p^{+}_{0}=p^{+}_{1}+q^{+}}\;.

In the above equation,
x_{p}=p_{0}^{+}/p_{p}^{+}
is the plus-momentum
fraction of the quark with respect to its parent (projectile) proton.

As already mentioned, we work in the dipole picture, formulated in
LCPT. In this approach, the projectile dynamics take place on a much
shorter timescale than those of the target. Therefore, on the partonic
level the projectile can be described by a Fock state with perturbatively
calculable dynamics, interacting with a static external potential
provided by the target. The amplitudes
\mathcal{M}
are defined
as follows:

\displaystyle\,{}_{f}\big\langle\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{1})\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{*}(\vec{q})\big|\hat{F}-1\big|\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{0})\big\rangle_{i}

\displaystyle=2\pi\delta(p_{0}^{+}-p_{1}^{+}-q^{+})\mathcal{M}\;,

(7)

where the external potential
\hat{F}
(
-1
for reasons of unitarity)
is evaluated between the Fock states of the incoming (
i
) quark,
and outgoing (
f
) quark together with the virtual photon. The first step is
to calculate the perturbative evolution of the asymptotic Fock states
to and from the position of the shockwave at
x^{+}=0
. Indeed, the
two interacting or ‘dressed’ Fock states
|...\rangle_{i,f}

are related to eigenstates
|...\rangle
of the free Hamiltonian
through the time evolution:

\displaystyle\big|\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{0})\big\rangle_{i}

\displaystyle=\hat{\mathcal{U}}(-\infty,0)\big|\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{0})\big\rangle\;,

(8)

\displaystyle\,{}_{f}\big\langle\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{1})\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{*}(\vec{q})\big|

\displaystyle=\big\langle\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{1})\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{*}(\vec{q})\big|\hat{\mathcal{U}}(0,+\infty)\;,

where
\hat{\mathcal{U}}
is the LC-time evolution operator defined
as:

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{U}}(a,b)

\displaystyle\equiv\hat{T}\exp\Bigl(-i\int_{a}^{b}\mathrm{d}x^{+}\hat{\mathcal{H}}(x^{+})\Bigr)\;,

(9)

with
\hat{T}
the (LC)-time ordering operator and
\hat{\mathcal{H}}(x^{+})

the LC Hamiltonian. In the interaction picture, the time-evolution
of the latter is given by:

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{H}}(x^{+})

\displaystyle=e^{i\hat{H}_{0}x^{+}}\hat{V}e^{-i\hat{H}_{0}x^{+}}\;,

(10)

with
\hat{H}_{0}
the free Hamiltonian, and
\hat{V}
the collection
of interaction terms. Acting with (
9
) on the Fock
state
\big|\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{0})\big\rangle
, which is per definition
an eigenstate of the free Hamiltonian with
\hat{H}_{0}\big|\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{0})\big\rangle=p_{0}^{-}\big|\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{0})\big\rangle
,
we obtain:

\displaystyle\big|\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{0})\big\rangle_{i}

\displaystyle=\hat{\mathcal{U}}(-\infty,0)\big|\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{0})\big\rangle

(11)

\displaystyle=\big|\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{0})\big\rangle-i\int\mathrm{PS}(\vec{\ell},\vec{k})\int_{-\infty}^{0}\mathrm{d}x^{+}\big|\mathbf{q}(\vec{\ell})\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{*}(\vec{k})\big\rangle

\displaystyle\times\big\langle\mathbf{q}(\vec{\ell})\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{*}(\vec{k})\big|e^{i(\ell^{-}+k^{-})x^{+}}\hat{V}e^{-ip_{0}^{-}x^{+}}\big|\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{0})\big\rangle

\displaystyle=\big|\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{0})\big\rangle+\int\mathrm{PS}(\vec{\ell},\vec{k})\frac{\big\langle\mathbf{q}(\vec{\ell})\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{*}(\vec{k})\big|\hat{V}\big|\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{0})\big\rangle}{p_{0}^{-}-\ell^{-}-k^{-}+i0^{+}}\big|\mathbf{q}(\vec{\ell})\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{*}(\vec{k})\big\rangle\;,

up to the perturbative order we are interested in, and where we have
introduced the notation:

\int\mathrm{PS}(\vec{\ell})=\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{D-1}\vec{\ell}\,\theta(\ell^{+})}{(2\pi)^{D-1}2\ell^{+}}\;.

(12)

Likewise, at leading order:

\displaystyle\big\langle\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{1})\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{\lambda}^{*}(\vec{q})\big|\hat{\mathcal{U}}(0,+\infty)=\big\langle\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{1})\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{\lambda}^{*}(\vec{q})\big|

(13)

\displaystyle+\int\mathrm{PS}(\vec{\ell})\big\langle\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{1})\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{\lambda}^{*}(\vec{q})\big|\exp\Bigl(-i\int_{0}^{+\infty}\mathrm{d}x^{+}\hat{\mathcal{H}}(x^{+})\Bigr)\big|\mathbf{q}(\vec{\ell})\big\rangle\big\langle\mathbf{q}(\vec{\ell})\big|\;,

\displaystyle=\big\langle\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{1})\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{\lambda}^{*}(\vec{q})\big|+\int\mathrm{PS}(\vec{\ell})\frac{\big\langle\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{1})\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{\lambda}^{*}(\vec{q})\big|\hat{V}\big|\mathbf{q}(\vec{\ell})\big\rangle}{p_{1}^{+}+q^{-}-\ell_{0}^{-}+i0^{+}}\big\langle\mathbf{q}(\vec{\ell})\big|\;.

Combining (
7
), (
11
), and (
13
),
we obtain:

\displaystyle{}_{f}\big\langle\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{1})\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{\lambda}^{*}(\vec{q})\big|\hat{F}-1\big|\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{0})\big\rangle_{i}=\big\langle\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{1})\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{\lambda}^{*}(\vec{q})\big|\hat{F}-1\big|\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{0})\big\rangle

(14)

\displaystyle+\int\mathrm{PS}(\vec{\ell})\frac{\big\langle\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{1})\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{\lambda}^{*}(\vec{q})\big|\hat{V}\big|\mathbf{q}(\vec{\ell})\big\rangle}{p_{1}^{-}+q^{-}-\ell^{-}+i0^{+}}\big\langle\mathbf{q}(\vec{\ell})\big|\hat{F}-1\big|\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{0})\big\rangle

\displaystyle+\int\mathrm{PS}(\vec{\ell},\vec{k})\frac{\big\langle\mathbf{q}(\vec{\ell})\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{\lambda}^{*}(\vec{k})\big|\hat{V}\big|\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{0})\big\rangle}{p_{0}^{-}-\ell^{-}-k^{-}+i0^{+}}\big\langle\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{1})\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{\lambda}^{*}(\vec{q})\big|\hat{F}-1\big|\mathbf{q}(\vec{\ell})\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{*}(\vec{k})\big\rangle\;.

In the eikonal approximation we are using, the potential
\hat{F}

can never change the particle content of the Fock states, which are
orthogonal. The first line in the above equation, therefore, disappears.
The second and third lines correspond to the first and second diagram
in figure
1
, respectively. In the former, the incoming
quark scatters off the external potential after which it emits a virtual
photon. In the latter, the photon is emitted before the interaction
with the potential. We will now evaluate eq. (
14
)
further, starting with the perturbative interaction terms. The relevant
piece of the interaction part of the QED LC Hamiltonian is, with the
short-hand notations
\int\mathrm{d}^{D-3}\vec{x}=\mu^{4-D}\int\mathrm{d}x^{-}\mathrm{d}^{D-2}\mathbf{x}

and
\vec{x}=(x^{-},\mathbf{x})
:

\displaystyle\hat{V}

\displaystyle=\int\mathrm{d}^{D-3}\vec{x}:g_{\mathrm{em}}\bar{\psi}(\vec{x})\cancel{A}(\vec{x})\psi(\vec{x}):\;,

(15)

where
:\;:
denotes normal ordering. Using the definitions of the
fermion- and boson fields in terms of the creation- and annihilation
operators:

\displaystyle\psi(\vec{x})

\displaystyle=\int\mathrm{PS}(\vec{k})\big(e^{-i\vec{k}\cdot\vec{x}}u(\vec{k})b_{\vec{k}}+e^{i\vec{k}\cdot\vec{x}}v(\vec{k})d_{\vec{k}}^{\dagger}\big)\;,

(16)

\displaystyle\cancel{A}(\vec{x})

\displaystyle=\int\mathrm{PS}(\vec{k})\big(e^{i\vec{k}\cdot\vec{x}}\cancel{\epsilon}^{*}(\vec{k})a_{\vec{k}}^{\dagger}+e^{-i\vec{k}\cdot\vec{x}}\cancel{\epsilon}(\vec{k})a_{\vec{k}}\big)\;,

it is then a straightforward exercise to show that:

\displaystyle\big\langle\mathbf{q}(\vec{\ell})\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{\lambda}^{*}(\vec{k})\big|\hat{V}\big|\mathbf{q}(\vec{p})\big\rangle

\displaystyle=\big\langle 0\big|b_{\vec{\ell}}\,a_{\vec{k}}^{\lambda}\,\hat{V}\,b_{\vec{p}}^{\dagger}\big|0\big\rangle\;,

(17)

\displaystyle=(2\pi)^{D-1}\delta^{(D-1)}\big(\vec{p}-\vec{\ell}-\vec{k}\big)g_{\mathrm{em}}\bar{u}(\vec{\ell})\cancel{\epsilon}_{\lambda}^{*}(\vec{k})u(\vec{p})\;.

We note that the Fock states are normalized the usual way with the
help of the (anti)-commutation relations:

\displaystyle\{b_{\vec{k}},b_{\vec{p}}^{\dagger}\}

\displaystyle=\{d_{\vec{k}},d_{\vec{p}}^{\dagger}\}=(2k^{+})(2\pi)^{D-1}\delta^{(D-1)}(\vec{k}-\vec{p})\;,

(18)

\displaystyle{}[a_{\vec{k}},a_{\vec{p}}^{\dagger}]

\displaystyle=(2k^{+})(2\pi)^{D-1}\delta^{(D-1)}(\vec{k}-\vec{p})\;,

such that, demanding that for empty states
\langle 0|0\rangle=1
,
it follows that, e.g.:

\displaystyle\big\langle\mathbf{q}(\vec{\ell}_{2})\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{*}(\vec{k}_{2})\big|\mathbf{q}(\vec{\ell}_{1})\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{*}(\vec{k}_{1})\big\rangle

\displaystyle=\!2k_{1}^{+}(2\pi)^{D\!-\!1}\delta^{(D\!-\!1)}(\vec{k}_{1}\!-\!\vec{k}_{2})2\ell_{1}^{+}(2\pi)^{D\!-\!1}\delta^{(D\!-\!1)}(\vec{\ell}_{1}\!-\!\vec{\ell}_{2})\,.

(19)

In order not to clutter the formulas, we will always suppress spinor
indices, fundamental color indices, and fermion helicity indices.

We now proceed by simplifying the Dirac algebra in (
14
).
Keeping the anticommutation relation
\{\gamma^{\mu},\gamma^{\nu}\}=2g^{\mu\nu}

in mind, we introduce the projectors
\mathcal{P}_{G}=\gamma^{-}\gamma^{+}/2

and
\mathcal{P}_{B}=\gamma^{+}\gamma^{-}/2
which allow us to define
‘good’ and ‘bad’ spinor components:

\displaystyle u(\vec{k})

\displaystyle=\big(\mathcal{P}_{G}+\mathcal{P}_{B}\big)u(\vec{k})\equiv u_{G}(\vec{k})+u_{B}(\vec{k})\;,

(20)

\displaystyle\bar{u}(\vec{k})

\displaystyle=\vec{u}(\vec{k})\big(\mathcal{P}_{G}+\mathcal{P}_{B}\big)\equiv\bar{u}_{B}(\vec{k})+\bar{u}_{G}(\vec{k})\;.

The same definitions hold for the antifermion spinors
v(\vec{k})
.
The Dirac equation
\cancel{k}u(\vec{k})=0
then introduces a dependence
between both components:

\displaystyle u_{B}(\vec{k})

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma^{+}}{2k^{+}}\mathbf{k}\cdot\boldsymbol{\gamma}\,u_{G}(k^{+})\;,

(21)

with exactly the same equation holding for the antifermion spinors,
at least in the massless case. Note that the good spinors only depend
on the plus-momentum component.

Relation (
21
) allows us to rewrite a generic spinor
product
\bar{u}(\vec{k}_{1})\cancel{\epsilon}_{\lambda}(\vec{k}_{3})u(\vec{k}_{2})

as:

\displaystyle\bar{u}(\vec{k}_{1})\cancel{\epsilon}_{\lambda}(\vec{k}_{3})u(\vec{k}_{2})

(22)

\displaystyle=\bar{u}_{G}(k_{1}^{+})\Big(1\!+\!\frac{\mathbf{k}_{1}\!\cdot\!\boldsymbol{\gamma}\,\gamma^{+}}{2k_{1}^{+}}\Big)\Big(\gamma^{+}\epsilon_{\lambda}^{-}(\vec{k}_{3})\!+\!\gamma^{-}\epsilon_{\lambda}^{+}(\vec{k}_{3})\!-\!\boldsymbol{\gamma}\!\cdot\!\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{\lambda}(\vec{k}_{3})\Big)\Big(\frac{\gamma^{+}\,\mathbf{k}_{2}\!\cdot\!\boldsymbol{\gamma}}{2k_{2}^{+}}\!+\!1\Big)\,u_{G}(k_{2}^{+})\,.

Applying the above identity to the spinor product in (
17
),
using the polarization vectors (
4
), we find in the
transverse case:

\displaystyle\big\langle\mathbf{q}(\vec{\ell})\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{\lambda}^{*}(\vec{k})\big|\hat{V}\big|\mathbf{q}(\vec{p})\big\rangle

\displaystyle=g_{\mathrm{em}}(2\pi)^{D-1}\delta^{(D-1)}\big(\vec{p}-\vec{\ell}-\vec{k}\big)

(23)

\displaystyle\times\bar{u}_{G}(\ell^{+})\gamma^{+}\Big(\frac{\mathbf{k}^{\lambda}}{k^{+}}+\frac{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\bar{\lambda}}}{2\ell^{+}}\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{\bar{\lambda}}\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{\lambda}+\frac{\mathbf{p}^{\bar{\lambda}}}{2p^{+}}\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{\lambda}\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{\bar{\lambda}}\Big)\,u_{G}(p^{+})\;,

since from
\{\gamma^{\mu},\gamma^{\nu}\}=2g^{\mu\nu}
it follows
that
\gamma^{+}\gamma^{+}=0
and
\{\gamma^{\pm},\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{i}\}=0
.
For this same reason, it is easy to see that
\bar{u}_{G}\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{i}u_{G}=0
.
Finally, for the momentum configurations in (
14
),
we end up with:

\displaystyle\big\langle\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{1})\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{\lambda}^{*}(\vec{q})\big|\hat{V}\big|\mathbf{q}(\vec{\ell})\big\rangle

\displaystyle=g_{\mathrm{em}}(2\pi)^{D-1}\delta^{(D-1)}\big(\vec{p}_{1}+\vec{q}-\vec{\ell}\big)

(24)

\displaystyle\times\frac{p_{1}^{+}\mathbf{q}^{\bar{\lambda}}-q^{+}\mathbf{p}_{1}^{\bar{\lambda}}}{2p_{1}^{+}(p_{1}^{+}+q^{+})}\bar{u}_{G}(p_{1}^{+})\gamma^{+}\Big[\Big(1+\frac{2p_{1}^{+}}{q^{+}}\Big)\delta^{\lambda\bar{\lambda}}-i\sigma^{\lambda\bar{\lambda}}\Big]u_{G}(\ell^{+})\;,

and:

\displaystyle\big\langle\mathbf{q}(\vec{\ell})\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{\lambda}^{*}(\vec{k})\big|\hat{V}\big|\mathbf{q}(\vec{p})\big\rangle

\displaystyle=g_{\mathrm{em}}(2\pi)^{D-1}\delta^{(D-1)}\big(\vec{p}-\vec{\ell}-\vec{k}\big)

(25)

\displaystyle\times\frac{-\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\bar{\lambda}}}{2\ell^{+}}\bar{u}_{G}(\ell^{+})\gamma^{+}\Big[\Big(1+\frac{2\ell^{+}}{k^{+}}\Big)\delta^{\lambda\bar{\lambda}}-i\sigma^{\lambda\bar{\lambda}}\Big]u_{G}(p^{+})\;,

In the above formulas, we replaced the products of two transverse
gamma matrices with:

\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{i}\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{j}=-\delta^{ij}-i\sigma^{ij}\;,

(26)

where the Dirac sigma defined as
\sigma^{ij}=(i/2)[\gamma^{i},\gamma^{j}]
.

Let us now turn to the interaction of the projectile with the target.
This interaction is, as explained above, described by an external
potential. Moreover, the Color Glass Condensate approach asserts that,
in the eikonal approximation, this potential is built from Wilson
lines along the projectile direction
x^{+}
:

\displaystyle U_{\mathbf{x}}

\displaystyle\equiv\mathcal{P}\exp\Big(-ig_{s}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\mathrm{d}x^{+}A_{a}^{-}(x^{+},\mathbf{x})t^{a}\Big)\;,

(27)

\displaystyle W_{\mathbf{x}}

\displaystyle\equiv\mathcal{P}\exp\Big(-ig_{s}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\mathrm{d}x^{+}A_{a}^{-}(x^{+},\mathbf{x})T^{a}\Big)\;,

where
\mathcal{P}
is the path-ordering operator,
g_{s}
is the
strong coupling,
A_{a}^{-}
the minus-component of the gluon field,
and where
t^{a}
and
T^{a}
are the generators of
\mathrm{SU}(N_{c})

in the fundamental and adjoint representation, respectively. The precise
form of the potential depends on the Fock state. In transverse coordinate
space, each (anti)quark and gluon in the projectile adds a Wilson
line to the potential that inherits the quark or gluon transverse
coordinate and color representation. In the present case, the projectile-target
interactions in (
14
) are given by:

\displaystyle\big\langle\mathbf{q}(\vec{\ell})\big|\hat{F}-1\big|\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{0})\big\rangle

\displaystyle=2p_{0}^{+}2\pi\delta(p_{0}^{+}-\ell^{+})\int_{\mathbf{x}}e^{-i\mathbf{x}\cdot\boldsymbol{\ell}}\big(U_{\mathbf{x}}-1\big)\;,

(28)

\displaystyle\big\langle\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{1})\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{*}(\vec{q})\big|\hat{F}\!-\!1\big|\mathbf{q}(\vec{\ell})\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{*}(\vec{k})\big\rangle

\displaystyle=2p_{1}^{+}2\pi\delta(p_{1}^{+}-\ell^{+})2q^{+}2\pi\delta(q^{+}-k^{+})

\displaystyle\times\int_{\mathbf{x}}e^{-i\mathbf{x}\cdot(\mathbf{p}_{1}-\mathbf{k})}\big(U_{\mathbf{x}}-1\big)\;,

where we introduced the short-hand notation
\int_{\mathbf{\mathbf{x}}}=\int\mathrm{d}^{D-2}\mathbf{x}

for the integration over
D-2
transverse coordinate space. Moreover,
in the present eikonal approximation, the interaction preserves plus-momentum
and the helicity. Combining (
7
), (
14
),
(
24
), (
25
), and (
28
),
we can finally write down the expressions for the leading-order amplitudes:

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LO1}}^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\frac{-g_{\mathrm{em}}q^{+}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{\bar{\lambda}}}{p_{0}^{+}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{2}+p_{1}^{+}M^{2}}\bar{u}_{G}(p_{1}^{+})\gamma^{+}\mathrm{Dirac}^{\lambda\bar{\lambda}}\big(1+\frac{2p_{1}^{+}}{q^{+}}\big)u_{G}(p_{0}^{+})\int_{\mathbf{x}}e^{-i\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\mathbf{x}}\big(U_{\mathbf{x}}-1\big)\;,

(29)

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LO2}}^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\frac{-g_{\mathrm{em}}q^{+}\mathbf{q}^{\bar{\lambda}}}{p_{0}^{+}\mathbf{q}^{2}+p_{1}^{+}M^{2}}\bar{u}_{G}(p_{1}^{+})\gamma^{+}\mathrm{Dirac}^{\lambda\bar{\lambda}}\big(1+\frac{2p_{1}^{+}}{q^{+}}\big)u_{G}(p_{0}^{+})\int_{\mathbf{x}}e^{-i\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\mathbf{x}}\big(U_{\mathbf{x}}-1\big)\;,

where we have introduced the following momentum combinations, which will be
used throughout this work:

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}_{\perp}

\displaystyle\equiv\frac{q^{+}\mathbf{p}_{1}-p_{1}^{+}\mathbf{q}}{p_{0}^{+}}\qquad\mathrm{and}\qquad\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\equiv\mathbf{p}_{1}+\mathbf{q}\;,

(30)

as well as the definition:

\displaystyle\mathrm{Dirac}^{\lambda\bar{\lambda}}(\xi)

\displaystyle\equiv\xi\delta^{\lambda\bar{\lambda}}\mathds{1}_{4}-i\sigma^{\lambda\bar{\lambda}}\;.

(31)

A similar calculation for the longitudinally polarized virtual photon
gives:

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LO1}}^{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{g_{\mathrm{em}}}{M}\frac{p_{0}^{+}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{2}-p_{1}^{+}M^{2}}{p_{0}^{+}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{2}+p_{1}^{+}M^{2}}\bar{u}_{G}(p_{1}^{+})\gamma^{+}u_{G}(p_{0}^{+})\int_{\mathbf{x}}e^{-i\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\mathbf{x}}\big(U_{\mathbf{x}}-1\big)\;,

(32)

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LO2}}^{0}

\displaystyle=-\frac{g_{\mathrm{em}}}{M}\frac{p_{0}^{+}\mathbf{q}^{2}-p_{1}^{+}M^{2}}{p_{0}^{+}\mathbf{q}^{2}+p_{1}^{+}M^{2}}\bar{u}_{G}(p_{1}^{+})\gamma^{+}u_{G}(p_{0}^{+})\int_{\mathbf{x}}e^{-i\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\mathbf{x}}\big(U_{\mathbf{x}}-1\big)\;.

Multiplying the amplitudes (
29
) and (
32
) with
their complex conjugate, and summing over the polarization indices

\lambda
of the transversely polarized virtual photon or vector
boson, we arrive at:

\displaystyle\sum_{\lambda}\big|\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LO1}}^{\lambda}+\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LO2}}^{\lambda}\big|^{2}

\displaystyle=g_{\mathrm{em}}^{2}N_{c}\Big(\frac{q^{+}\mathbf{q}}{p_{0}^{+}\mathbf{q}^{2}+p_{1}^{+}M^{2}}+\frac{q^{+}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}}{p_{0}^{+}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{2}+p_{1}^{+}M^{2}}\Big)^{2}|\mathrm{Dirac}_{\mathrm{LO}}^{\mathrm{T}}|^{2}

(33)

\displaystyle\times\int_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}e^{-i\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{x}^{\prime})}\big(s_{\mathbf{x}\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}+1\big)\;,

and:

\displaystyle|\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LO1}}^{0}+\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LO2}}^{0}|^{2}

\displaystyle=g_{\mathrm{em}}^{2}\frac{N_{c}}{M^{2}}\Big(\frac{p_{0}^{+}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{2}-p_{1}^{+}M^{2}}{p_{0}^{+}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{2}+p_{1}^{+}M^{2}}-\frac{p_{0}^{+}\mathbf{q}^{2}-p_{1}^{+}M^{2}}{p_{0}^{+}\mathbf{q}^{2}+p_{1}^{+}M^{2}}\Big)^{2}|\mathrm{Dirac}_{\mathrm{LO}}^{0}|^{2}

(34)

\displaystyle\times\int_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}e^{-i\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{x}^{\prime})}\big(s_{\mathbf{x}\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}+1\big)\;.

In the above results, we used
s_{\mathbf{x}\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}

to denote the dipole color operator:

s_{\mathbf{x}\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}=\frac{1}{N_{c}}\mathrm{Tr}\big(U_{\mathbf{x}}U_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}^{\dagger}\big)\;,

(35)

where we take the trace over the fundamental
\mathrm{SU}(N_{c})

color indices (which we do not write explicitly). The traces over
spinor indices are given by:

\displaystyle|\mathrm{Dirac}_{\mathrm{LO}}^{\mathrm{T}}|^{2}

(36)

\displaystyle\equiv\mathrm{Tr}\Bigg[\bar{u}_{G}(p_{0}^{+})\gamma^{+}\mathrm{Dirac}^{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}\big(1+\frac{2p_{1}^{+}}{q^{+}}\big)^{\dagger}u_{G}(p_{1}^{+})\bar{u}_{G}(p_{1}^{+})\gamma^{+}\mathrm{Dirac}^{\lambda\bar{\lambda}}\big(1+\frac{2p_{1}^{+}}{q^{+}}\big)u_{G}(p_{0}^{+})\Bigg]\;,

\displaystyle|\mathrm{Dirac}_{\mathrm{LO}}^{0}|^{2}\equiv\mathrm{Tr}\Bigg[\bar{u}_{G}(p_{0}^{+})\gamma^{+}u_{G}(p_{1}^{+})\bar{u}_{G}(p_{1}^{+})\gamma^{+}u_{G}(p_{0}^{+})\Bigg]\;.

Applying the cyclic permutation property of the trace as well as the
completeness relation (a summation over fermion spin is understood):

\displaystyle u_{G}(q^{+})\bar{u}_{G}(q^{+})\gamma^{+}

\displaystyle=2q^{+}\mathcal{P}_{G}\;,

(37)

we obtain:

\displaystyle|\mathrm{Dirac}_{\mathrm{LO}}^{\mathrm{T}}|^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Tr}\Bigg[2p_{0}^{+}\mathcal{P}_{G}\mathrm{Dirac}^{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}\big(1+\frac{2p_{1}^{+}}{q^{+}}\big)^{\dagger}2p_{1}^{+}\mathcal{P}_{G}\mathrm{Dirac}^{\lambda\bar{\lambda}}\big(1+\frac{2p_{1}^{+}}{q^{+}}\big)\Bigg]\;.

(38)

Since the projector
\mathcal{P}_{G}
commutes with transverse gamma
matrices, from which the structures
\mathrm{Dirac}^{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}

are built, the above expression further simplifies to:

\displaystyle|\mathrm{Dirac}_{\mathrm{LO}}^{\mathrm{T}}|^{2}

\displaystyle=4p_{0}^{+}p_{1}^{+}\mathrm{Tr}\Bigg[\mathcal{P}_{G}\Big(\big(1\!+\!\frac{2p_{1}^{+}}{q^{+}}\big)\delta^{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}\mathds{1}_{4}\!+\!i\sigma^{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}\Big)\Big(\big(1\!+\!\frac{2p_{1}^{+}}{q^{+}}\big)\delta^{\lambda\bar{\lambda}}\mathds{1}_{4}\!-\!i\sigma^{\lambda\bar{\lambda}}\Big)\Bigg]\;,

(39)

\displaystyle=4p_{0}^{+}p_{1}^{+}\mathrm{Tr}\Bigg[\mathcal{P}_{G}\Big(\big(1+\frac{2p_{1}^{+}}{q^{+}}\big)^{2}\delta^{\lambda^{\prime}\bar{\lambda}}\mathds{1}_{4}+\sigma^{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}\sigma^{\lambda\bar{\lambda}}+2i\sigma^{\bar{\lambda}\lambda^{\prime}}\big(1+\frac{2p_{1}^{+}}{q^{+}}\big)\Big)\Bigg]\;.

Finally, with the help of the identities:

\displaystyle\sigma^{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}\sigma^{\lambda\bar{\lambda}}

\displaystyle=(D-3)\delta^{\lambda^{\prime}\bar{\lambda}}\mathds{1}_{4}+i(D-4)\sigma^{\lambda^{\prime}\bar{\lambda}}\;,

(40)

as well as:

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}\big(\mathcal{P}_{G}\big)

\displaystyle=2\;,\qquad\mathrm{Tr}\big(\mathcal{P}_{G}\sigma^{ij}\big)=0\;,

(41)

we end up with:

\displaystyle|\mathrm{Dirac}_{\mathrm{LO}}^{\mathrm{T}}|^{2}

\displaystyle=8p_{0}^{+}p_{1}^{+}\delta^{\lambda^{\prime}\bar{\lambda}}\Big(\big(1+\frac{2p_{1}^{+}}{q^{+}}\big)^{2}+D-3\Big)\;,

(42)

and similarly

\displaystyle|\mathrm{Dirac}_{\mathrm{LO}}^{0}|^{2}

\displaystyle=8p_{1}^{+}p_{0}^{+}\;.

(43)

In appendix
B
, the most important gamma-matrix
identities used in this work are collected.

Combining (
5
), (
33
), (
34
),
and (
43
), we eventually arrive at the following result
for the partonic cross sections:

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}\hat{\sigma}_{\mathrm{LO}}^{\mathrm{T}}}{\mathrm{d}z\mathrm{d}\bar{z}\mathrm{\mathrm{d}}^{2}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}\mathrm{\mathrm{d}}^{2}\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}

\displaystyle=\frac{g_{\mathrm{em}}^{2}N_{c}}{(2\pi)^{5}}\delta(1-z-\bar{z})\frac{1+(1-z)^{2}}{z}\Big(\frac{\mathbf{P}_{\perp}}{\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{2}+\bar{z}M^{2}}+\frac{\mathbf{q}}{\mathbf{q}^{2}+\bar{z}M^{2}}\Big)^{2}

(44)

\displaystyle\times\int_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}e^{-i\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{x}^{\prime})}\big(s_{\mathbf{x}\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}+1\big)\;,

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}\hat{\sigma}_{\mathrm{LO}}^{\mathrm{L}}}{\mathrm{d}z\mathrm{d}\bar{z}\mathrm{\mathrm{d}}^{2}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}\mathrm{\mathrm{d}}^{2}\mathbf{k}_{\perp}}

\displaystyle=\frac{g_{\mathrm{em}}^{2}N_{c}}{(2\pi)^{5}}\delta(1-z-\bar{z})\frac{1}{2zM^{2}}\Big(\frac{\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{2}-\bar{z}M^{2}}{\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{2}+\bar{z}M^{2}}-\frac{\mathbf{q}^{2}-\bar{z}M^{2}}{\mathbf{q}^{2}+\bar{z}M^{2}}\Big)^{2}

(45)

\displaystyle\times\int_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}e^{-i\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{x}^{\prime})}\big(s_{\mathbf{x}\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}+1\big)\;,

defining the momentum fractions
z\equiv q^{+}/p_{0}^{+}
and
\bar{z}=1-z
.

In the results (
44
) and (
45
), the
term containing the dipole
s_{\mathbf{x}\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}
quantifies
the potential provided by the target and experienced by the projectile.
However, it is built from Wilson lines that depend on the semi-classical
gauge fields
A^{-}
of the highly boosted target ‘shockwave’.
These fields carry the information on the target gluon structure,
which is at least partially nonperturbative. To indicate that, on
the hadronic level, the fields still need to be related to the target
properties, for instance using a model or by linking them to gluon
transverse momentum dependent PDFs, we write the above mentioned ‘target
average’
\langle...\rangle
. Likewise, the incoming quark can be
related to its parent proton by convolving the cross section with the
quark PDF, and a factor
\frac{\alpha_{\mathrm{em}}}{3\pi}\mathrm{d}\ln M^{2}

takes the
\gamma^{*}\to\ell^{+}\ell^{-}
splitting into account
in the simple scenario where only the total three-momentum and the invariant mass of the lepton
pair is measured
Berger:2001wr
. We finally obtain:

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}\sigma_{\mathrm{LO}}}{\mathrm{d}z\mathrm{d}\bar{z}\mathrm{\mathrm{d}}^{2}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}\mathrm{\mathrm{d}}^{2}\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\mathrm{d}\ln M^{2}}

(46)

\displaystyle=\frac{2\alpha_{\mathrm{em}}^{2}N_{c}}{3\pi}\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{4}}x_{p}f_{q}\big(x_{p},\mu^{2}\big)\Bigg[\frac{1+(1-z)^{2}}{z}\Big(\frac{\mathbf{P}_{\perp}}{\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{2}+\bar{z}M^{2}}+\frac{\mathbf{q}}{\mathbf{q}^{2}+\bar{z}M^{2}}\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2zM^{2}}\Big(\frac{\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{2}-\bar{z}M^{2}}{\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{2}+\bar{z}M^{2}}-\frac{\mathbf{q}^{2}-\bar{z}M^{2}}{\mathbf{q}^{2}+\bar{z}M^{2}}\Big)^{2}\Bigg]\int_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}e^{-i\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{x}^{\prime})}\big\langle s_{\mathbf{x}\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}+1\big\rangle\;,

in agreement with earlier results in the literature
Gelis:2002fw
;
Stasto:2012ru
.

Before turning to the next-to-leading order calculation, we remark
that, apart from the universal coupling constant
g_{\mathrm{em}}
, the amplitudes
(
29
) and (
32
) all contain a spinor structure

\bar{u}_{G}(p_{1}^{+})\gamma^{+}(...)u_{G}(p_{0}^{+})
. In fact,
this will also be true for all the NLO amplitudes. For ease of notation,
we therefore define the ‘reduced amplitudes’
\tilde{\mathcal{M}}
:

\mathcal{M}=g_{\mathrm{em}}\bar{u}_{G}(p_{1}^{+})\gamma^{+}\tilde{\mathcal{M}}u_{G}(p_{0}^{+})\;,

(47)

with which we will work in what follows. We also define, for later
convenience:

\displaystyle\mathrm{Dirac}_{\mathrm{LO}}^{\lambda\bar{\lambda}}

\displaystyle\equiv\mathrm{Dirac}^{\lambda\bar{\lambda}}\Big(1+\frac{2p_{1}^{+}}{q^{+}}\Big)\;.

(48)

3
Virtual next-to-leading order corrections

In this section, we review all the one-loop Feynman graphs for the
channel
q+A\to\gamma^{*}+q
. The corresponding amplitudes are presented
in general
D
dimensions, as many of them contain ultraviolet divergences
which will be regulated using dimensional regularization. The resulting
UV poles are subtracted from the amplitudes with the help of counterterms
that will, in turn, shown to cancel in the next section. Readers who
are interested how the amplitudes themselves are obtained within our
LCPT approach to the CGC are referred to appendix
C
,
where a representative amplitude is calculated from start to finish.

3.1
Self-energy corrections

Figure 2:
The four virtual contributions with a gluon loop on
the quark, which in two cases (
\mathrm{SE2}
and
\mathrm{SE3}
)
scatters off the shockwave. Similar one-loop corrections on the asymptotic
incoming or outgoing quark are treated separately in subsection
3.6
.

Diagram SE1

The reduced amplitude corresponding to a quark self-energy correction
after having hit the shockwave, but before the emission of the photon
(see fig.
2
), reads:

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathrm{SE1}}^{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha_{s}C_{F}}{D-2}\frac{1}{M}\frac{p_{0}^{+}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{2}-p_{1}^{+}M^{2}}{p_{0}^{+}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{2}+p_{1}^{+}M^{2}}\int_{k_{\mathrm{min}}^{+}}^{p_{0}^{+}}\frac{\mathrm{d}k^{+}}{k^{+}}\Big(\frac{k^{+}}{p_{0}^{+}}\Big)^{2}\mathrm{Dirac}_{\mathrm{SE}}^{jj}(p_{0}^{+})

(49)

\displaystyle\times\mathcal{A}_{0}(\Delta_{\mathrm{P}})\int_{\mathbf{x}}e^{-i\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\mathbf{x}}\big(U_{\mathbf{x}}-1\big)\;,

for a longitudinally polarized virtual photon, and

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathrm{SE1}}^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\alpha_{s}C_{F}}{D-2}\frac{q^{+}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{\bar{\lambda}}}{p_{0}^{+}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{2}+p_{1}^{+}M^{2}}\int_{k_{\mathrm{min}}^{+}}^{p_{0}^{+}}\frac{\mathrm{d}k^{+}}{k^{+}}\Big(\frac{k^{+}}{p_{0}^{+}}\Big)^{2}\mathrm{Dirac}_{\mathrm{LO}}^{\lambda\bar{\lambda}}\mathrm{Dirac}_{\mathrm{SE}}^{jj}(p_{0}^{+})

(50)

\displaystyle\times\mathcal{A}_{0}(\Delta_{\mathrm{P}})\int_{\mathbf{x}}e^{-i\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\mathbf{x}}\big(U_{\mathbf{x}}-1\big)\;,

when the photon is transversely polarized. In the above formulas,

C_{F}=(N_{c}^{2}-1)/2N_{c}
, and we defined the Dirac structures:

\displaystyle\mathrm{Dirac}_{\mathrm{SE}}^{\bar{\eta}\eta^{\prime}}(p_{0}^{+})

\displaystyle\equiv\Big[\big(1-\frac{2p_{0}^{+}}{k^{+}}\big)\delta^{\eta\bar{\eta}}\mathds{1}_{4}-i\sigma^{\eta\bar{\eta}}\Big]\Big[\big(\frac{2p_{0}^{+}}{k^{+}}-1\big)\delta^{\eta\eta^{\prime}}\mathds{1}_{4}-i\sigma^{\eta\eta^{\prime}}\Big]\;,

(51)

as well as:

\Delta_{\mathrm{P}}\equiv-\frac{k^{+}(p_{0}^{+}-k^{+})}{p_{0}^{+}p_{1}^{+}q^{+}}\big(p_{0}^{+}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{2}+p_{1}^{+}M^{2}\big)-i0^{+}\;.

(52)

At a later stage we will evaluate the
k^{+}
-integral and encounter
logarithmic branch cuts. Therefore, we keep track of the infinitesimal
imaginary part
i0^{+}
in the above expression, stemming from the
energy denominator from which this term stems.

The quantity
\mathcal{A}_{0}(\Delta_{\mathrm{P}})
in eqs. (
49
)
and (
50
) results from the integration over the gluon
transverse loop momentum, which we have evaluated using integral identity
eq. (
406
). As could be expected, the result is divergent
in the ultraviolet, and we use dimensional regularization with in

D=4-2\epsilon_{\mathrm{UV}}
to regulate it. Finally,
\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathrm{SE1}}^{\lambda}

and
\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathrm{SE1}}^{0}
diverge in the limit
of vanishing gluon plus-momentum
k^{+}\to 0
. This rapidity divergence
is regulated using a cutoff
k_{\mathrm{min}}^{+}
.

We now introduce a counterterm to cancel the UV-pole in expression
(
49
):

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathrm{SE1,UV}}^{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha_{s}C_{F}}{D-2}\frac{1}{M}\frac{p_{0}^{+}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{2}-p_{1}^{+}M^{2}}{p_{0}^{+}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{2}+p_{1}^{+}M^{2}}\int_{k_{\mathrm{min}}^{+}}^{p_{0}^{+}}\frac{\mathrm{d}k^{+}}{k^{+}}\Big(\frac{k^{+}}{p_{0}^{+}}\Big)^{2}\mathrm{Dirac}_{\mathrm{SE}}^{jj}(p_{0}^{+})

(53)

\displaystyle\times\mathcal{A}_{0}(\Delta_{\mathrm{UV}})\int_{\mathbf{x}}e^{-i\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\mathbf{x}}\big(U_{\mathbf{x}}-1\big)\;.

The UV-subtracted amplitude can then be evaluated in
D=4
dimensions:

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathrm{SE1,sub}}^{0}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathrm{LO1}}^{0}\frac{\alpha_{s}C_{F}}{\pi}\int_{k_{\mathrm{min}}^{+}}^{p_{0}^{+}}\frac{\mathrm{d}k^{+}}{8k^{+}}\Big(\frac{k^{+}}{p_{0}^{+}}\Big)^{2}\mathrm{Dirac}_{\mathrm{SE}}^{jj}(p_{0}^{+})\ln\frac{\Delta_{\mathrm{UV}}}{\Delta_{\mathrm{P}}}

(54)

\displaystyle=\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathrm{LO1}}^{0}\frac{\alpha_{s}C_{F}}{\pi}\int_{k_{\mathrm{min}}^{+}}^{p_{0}^{+}}\frac{\mathrm{d}k^{+}}{4k^{+}}\Big(\frac{k^{+}}{p_{0}^{+}}\Big)^{2}\Big(\big(1-\frac{2p_{0}^{+}}{k^{+}}\big)^{2}+1\Big)\ln\frac{\Delta_{\mathrm{P}}}{\Delta_{\mathrm{UV}}}\;.

The exact same steps we just took can be followed in the transversely
polarized case, defining a counterterm:

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathrm{SE1,UV}}^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\alpha_{s}C_{F}}{D-2}\frac{q^{+}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{\bar{\lambda}}}{p_{0}^{+}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{2}+p_{1}^{+}M^{2}}\int_{k_{\mathrm{min}}^{+}}^{p_{0}^{+}}\frac{\mathrm{d}k^{+}}{k^{+}}\Big(\frac{k^{+}}{p_{0}^{+}}\Big)^{2}\mathrm{Dirac}_{\mathrm{LO}}^{\lambda\bar{\lambda}}\mathrm{Dirac}_{\mathrm{SE}}^{jj}(p_{0}^{+})

(55)

\displaystyle\times\mathcal{A}_{0}(\Delta_{\mathrm{UV}})\int_{\mathbf{x}}e^{-i\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\mathbf{x}}\big(U_{\mathbf{x}}-1\big)\;,

such that the UV-subtracted amplitude can be evaluated in
D=4
dimensions,
yielding:

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathrm{SE1,sub}}^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathrm{LO1}}^{\lambda}\frac{\alpha_{s}C_{F}}{\pi}\int_{k_{\mathrm{min}}^{+}}^{p_{0}^{+}}\frac{\mathrm{d}k^{+}}{4k^{+}}\Big(\frac{k^{+}}{p_{0}^{+}}\Big)^{2}\Big(\big(1-\frac{2p_{0}^{+}}{k^{+}}\big)^{2}+1\Big)\ln\frac{\Delta_{\mathrm{P}}}{\Delta_{\mathrm{UV}}}\;.

(56)

Comparing the above result with (
54
), it is clear
that the loop correction calculated here is independent of the polarization
of the virtual photon.

Diagram SE2

Using the expressions (
400
) and (
401
)
for the so-called Weizsäcker-Williams fields
A^{i}(\mathbf{x})

and
A^{i}(\mathbf{x},\Delta)
, as well as the property (
402
),
one obtains for the diagrams where the quark self-energy loop scatters
off the shockwave before emitting a virtual photon (see fig.
2
):

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathrm{SE2}}^{0}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\alpha_{s}}{D-2}\frac{1}{M}\frac{p_{0}^{+}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{2}-p_{1}^{+}M^{2}}{p_{0}^{+}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{2}+p_{1}^{+}M^{2}}\int_{k_{\mathrm{min}}^{+}}^{p_{0}^{+}}\frac{\mathrm{d}k^{+}}{k^{+}}\Big(\frac{k^{+}}{p_{0}^{+}}\Big)^{2}\mathrm{Dirac}_{\mathrm{SE}}^{jj}(p_{0}^{+})

(57)

\displaystyle\times\int_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{z}}A^{i}(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{z})A^{i}\big(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{z},\Delta_{\mathrm{P}}\big)

\displaystyle\times e^{-i\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\Big(\frac{p_{0}^{+}-k^{+}}{p_{0}^{+}}\mathbf{x}+\frac{k^{+}}{p_{0}^{+}}\mathbf{z}\Big)}\big(t^{c}U_{\mathbf{x}}U_{\mathbf{z}}^{\dagger}t^{c}U_{\mathbf{z}}-C_{F}\big)\;,

and:

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathrm{SE2}}^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha_{s}}{D-2}\frac{q^{+}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{\bar{\lambda}}}{p_{0}^{+}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{2}+p_{1}^{+}M^{2}}\int_{k_{\mathrm{min}}^{+}}^{p_{0}^{+}}\frac{\mathrm{d}k^{+}}{k^{+}}\Big(\frac{k^{+}}{p_{0}^{+}}\Big)^{2}\mathrm{Dirac}_{\mathrm{LO}}^{\lambda\bar{\lambda}}\mathrm{Dirac}_{\mathrm{SE}}^{jj}(p_{0}^{+})

(58)

\displaystyle\times\int_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{z}}A^{i}(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{z})A^{i}\big(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{z},\Delta_{\mathrm{P}}\big)

\displaystyle\times e^{-i\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\Big(\frac{p_{0}^{+}-k^{+}}{p_{0}^{+}}\mathbf{x}+\frac{k^{+}}{p_{0}^{+}}\mathbf{z}\Big)}\big(t^{c}U_{\mathbf{x}}U_{\mathbf{z}}^{\dagger}t^{c}U_{\mathbf{z}}-C_{F}\big)\;.

Note that we have used the Fierz identity
t^{a}W^{ab}=U^{\dagger}t^{b}U

to transform Wilson lines
W
in the adjoint representation, which
parameterize the virtual gluon interacting with the shockwave, into
the usual Wilson lines
U
in the fundamental representation (
27
).

In the limit
\mathbf{z}\to\mathbf{x}
, or equivalently when the
virtual gluon obtains an infinitely large transverse momentum, the
amplitudes (
58
) and (
57
) exhibit an UV divergence.
Indeed, we have from definitions (
400
) and (
401
):

\displaystyle A^{i}(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{z})A^{i}(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{z},\Delta)

(59)

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbf{k},\boldsymbol{\ell}}e^{-i(\mathbf{k}+\boldsymbol{\ell})\cdot(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{z})}\frac{\mathbf{k}^{i}}{\mathbf{k}^{2}}\frac{\mathbf{\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{i}}{\mathbf{\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{2}+\Delta}\;,

\displaystyle=-\frac{\mu^{2(4-D)}}{2(2\pi^{2})^{\frac{D-2}{2}}}\frac{1}{|\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{z}|^{\frac{3D-10}{4}}}\Gamma(\frac{D-2}{2})(\sqrt{\Delta})^{\frac{D-2}{2}}\mathrm{K}_{\frac{D-2}{2}}\big(|\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{z}|\sqrt{\Delta}\big)\;,

which is singular for
\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{z}\to 0
but tends to zero
for
\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{z}\to\infty
. Moreover, since
\lim_{\Delta\to 0}\sqrt{\Delta}\mathrm{K}_{1}\big(\sqrt{\Delta}\big)=1
,
there are no extra divergences generated when integrating over
k^{+}

(since
\Delta_{\mathrm{P}}(k^{+}\to p_{0}^{+})\to 0
).

A counterterm for this UV divergence can be constructed from (
58
)
and (
57
) by setting
\mathbf{z}=\mathbf{x}
everywhere
except in the singular part, after which the integral over
\mathbf{z}

can be evaluated by reverting to momentum space and using the standard
result (
406
):

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathrm{SE2,UV}}^{0}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\alpha_{s}C_{F}}{D-2}\frac{1}{M}\frac{p_{0}^{+}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{2}-p_{1}^{+}M^{2}}{p_{0}^{+}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{2}+p_{1}^{+}M^{2}}\int_{k_{\mathrm{min}}^{+}}^{p_{0}^{+}}\frac{\mathrm{d}k^{+}}{k^{+}}\Big(\frac{k^{+}}{p_{0}^{+}}\Big)^{2}\mathrm{Dirac}_{\mathrm{SE}}^{jj}(p_{0}^{+})

(60)

\displaystyle\times\mathcal{A}_{0}(\Delta_{\mathrm{UV}})\int_{\mathbf{x}}e^{-i\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\mathbf{x}}\big(U_{\mathbf{x}}-1\big)=-\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathrm{SE1,UV}}^{0}\;,

and similarly for the transverse case:

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathrm{SE2,UV}}^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=-\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathrm{SE1,\mathrm{UV}}}^{\lambda}\;.

(61)

Therefore, the UV-counterterms of the amplitudes
\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathrm{SE2}}^{\lambda,0}

happen to be the exact opposite of
\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathrm{SE1}}^{\lambda,0}

(
50
).

The UV-subtracted amplitudes read:

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathrm{SE2,sub}}^{0}

\displaystyle=\alpha_{s}\frac{1}{M}\frac{p_{0}^{+}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{2}-p_{1}^{+}M^{2}}{p_{0}^{+}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{2}+p_{1}^{+}M^{2}}\int_{k_{\mathrm{min}}^{+}}^{p_{0}^{+}}\frac{\mathrm{d}k^{+}}{k^{+}}\Big(\frac{k^{+}}{p_{0}^{+}}\Big)^{2}\Big(\big(1-\frac{2p_{0}^{+}}{k^{+}}\big)^{2}+1\Big)

(62)

\displaystyle\times\Bigg[\int_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{z}}A^{i}(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{z})A^{i}\big(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{z},\Delta_{\mathrm{P}}\big)e^{-i\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\Big(\frac{p_{0}^{+}-k^{+}}{p_{0}^{+}}\mathbf{x}+\frac{k^{+}}{p_{0}^{+}}\mathbf{z}\Big)}\big(t^{c}U_{\mathbf{x}}U_{\mathbf{z}}^{\dagger}t^{c}U_{\mathbf{z}}-C_{F}\big)

\displaystyle-\mathcal{A}_{0}(\Delta_{\mathrm{UV}})\int_{\mathbf{x}}e^{-i\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\mathbf{x}}C_{F}\big(U_{\mathbf{x}}-1\big)\Bigg]\;,

and:

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathrm{SE2,sub}}^{\lambda}

(63)

\displaystyle=-\alpha_{s}\frac{q^{+}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{\bar{\lambda}}}{p_{0}^{+}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{2}+p_{1}^{+}M^{2}}\mathrm{Dirac}^{\lambda\bar{\lambda}}\big(1+2\frac{p_{1}^{+}}{q^{+}}\big)\int_{k_{\mathrm{min}}^{+}}^{p_{0}^{+}}\frac{\mathrm{d}k^{+}}{k^{+}}\Big(\frac{k^{+}}{p_{0}^{+}}\Big)^{2}\Big(\big(1-\frac{2p_{0}^{+}}{k^{+}}\big)^{2}+1\Big)

\displaystyle\times\Bigg[\int_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{z}}A^{i}(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{z})A^{i}\big(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{z},\Delta_{\mathrm{P}}\big)e^{-i\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\Big(\frac{p_{0}^{+}-k^{+}}{p_{0}^{+}}\mathbf{x}+\frac{k^{+}}{p_{0}^{+}}\mathbf{z}\Big)}\big(t^{c}U_{\mathbf{x}}U_{\mathbf{z}}^{\dagger}t^{c}U_{\mathbf{z}}-C_{F}\big)

\displaystyle-\mathcal{A}_{0}(\Delta_{\mathrm{UV}})\int_{\mathbf{x}}e^{-i\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\mathbf{x}}C_{F}\big(U_{\mathbf{x}}-1\big)\Bigg]\;.

Diagram SE3

This diagram is the counterpart of graph SE2, when the emission of
the virtual photon takes place before the quark self-energy loop (see
fig.
2
). The corresponding amplitudes read:

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathrm{SE3}}^{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha_{s}(-1)^{4-D}}{D-2}\frac{p_{0}^{+}\mathbf{q}^{2}-p_{1}^{+}M^{2}}{p_{0}^{+}\mathbf{q}^{2}+p_{1}^{+}M^{2}}\frac{1}{M}\int_{k_{\mathrm{min}}^{+}}^{p_{1}^{+}}\frac{\mathrm{d}k^{+}}{k^{+}}\Big(\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\Big)^{2}\mathrm{Dirac}_{\mathrm{SE}}^{jj}(p_{1}^{+})

(64)

\displaystyle\times\int_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{z}}A^{i}(\mathbf{z}-\mathbf{x})A^{i}(\mathbf{z}-\mathbf{x},\Delta_{\mathrm{q}})

\displaystyle\times e^{-i\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\Big(\frac{p_{1}^{+}-k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{x}+\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{z}\Big)}\big(t^{c}U_{\mathbf{x}}U_{\mathbf{z}}^{\dagger}t^{c}U_{\mathbf{z}}-C_{F}\big)\;,

and:

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathrm{SE3}}^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha_{s}(-1)^{4-D}}{D-2}\frac{q^{+}\mathbf{q}^{\bar{\lambda}}}{p_{0}^{+}\mathbf{q}^{2}+p_{1}^{+}M^{2}}\int_{k_{\mathrm{min}}^{+}}^{p_{1}^{+}}\frac{\mathrm{d}k^{+}}{k^{+}}\Big(\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\Big)^{2}\mathrm{Dirac}_{\mathrm{SE}}^{jj}(p_{1}^{+})\mathrm{Dirac}_{\mathrm{LO}}^{\lambda\bar{\lambda}}

(65)

\displaystyle\times\int_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{z}}A^{i}(\mathbf{z}-\mathbf{x})A^{i}(\mathbf{z}-\mathbf{x},\Delta_{\mathrm{q}})

\displaystyle\times e^{-i\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\Big(\frac{p_{1}^{+}-k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{x}+\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{z}\Big)}\big(t^{c}U_{\mathbf{x}}U_{\mathbf{z}}^{\dagger}t^{c}U_{\mathbf{z}}-C_{F}\big)\;.

In the above, we defined:

\displaystyle\Delta_{\mathrm{q}}

\displaystyle\equiv\frac{k^{+}(p_{1}^{+}-k^{+})}{(p_{1}^{+})^{2}q^{+}}(p_{0}^{+}\mathbf{q}^{2}+p_{1}^{+}M^{2})-i0^{+}\;.

(66)

Just like the amplitudes
\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathrm{SE2}}^{\lambda,0}
,
also (
65
) and (
64
) are UV-divergent in the
limit
\mathbf{z}\to\mathbf{x}
. Again, this divergence can be extracted
by setting
\mathbf{z}=\mathbf{x}
everywhere except in the singular
part, defining the counterterms:

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathrm{SE3,UV}}^{0}

\displaystyle=-\alpha_{s}C_{F}\frac{p_{0}^{+}\mathbf{q}^{2}-p_{1}^{+}M^{2}}{p_{0}^{+}\mathbf{q}^{2}+p_{1}^{+}M^{2}}\frac{1}{M}\int_{k_{\mathrm{min}}^{+}}^{p_{1}^{+}}\frac{\mathrm{d}k^{+}}{k^{+}}\Big(\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\Big)^{2}\Big(\big(1-\frac{2p_{1}^{+}}{k^{+}}\big)^{2}+D-3\Big)

(67)

\displaystyle\times\mathcal{A}_{0}(\Delta_{\mathrm{UV}})\int_{\mathbf{x}}e^{-i\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\mathbf{x}}\big(U_{\mathbf{x}}-1\big)\;,

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathrm{SE3,UV}}^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=-\alpha_{s}C_{F}\frac{q^{+}\mathbf{q}^{\bar{\lambda}}}{p_{0}^{+}\mathbf{q}^{2}+p_{1}^{+}M^{2}}\mathrm{Dirac}_{\mathrm{LO}}^{\lambda\bar{\lambda}}\int_{k_{\mathrm{min}}^{+}}^{p_{1}^{+}}\frac{\mathrm{d}k^{+}}{k^{+}}\Big(\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\Big)^{2}\Big(\big(1\!-\!\frac{2p_{1}^{+}}{k^{+}}\big)^{2}\!+\!D\!-\!3\Big)

\displaystyle\times\mathcal{A}_{0}(\Delta_{\mathrm{UV}})\int_{\mathbf{x}}e^{-i\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\mathbf{x}}\big(U_{\mathbf{x}}-1\big)\;.

The subtracted amplitudes then become:

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathrm{SE3,sub}}^{0}

\displaystyle=-\alpha_{s}\frac{p_{0}^{+}\mathbf{q}^{2}-p_{1}^{+}M^{2}}{p_{0}^{+}\mathbf{q}^{2}+p_{1}^{+}M^{2}}\frac{1}{M}\int_{k_{\mathrm{min}}^{+}}^{p_{1}^{+}}\frac{\mathrm{d}k^{+}}{k^{+}}\Big(\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\Big)^{2}\Big(\big(1-\frac{2p_{1}^{+}}{k^{+}}\big)^{2}+1\Big)

(68)

\displaystyle\times\Bigg[\int_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{z}}A^{i}(\mathbf{z}-\mathbf{x})A^{i}(\mathbf{z}-\mathbf{x},\Delta_{\mathrm{q}})e^{-i\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\Big(\frac{p_{1}^{+}-k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{x}+\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{z}\Big)}\big(t^{c}U_{\mathbf{x}}U_{\mathbf{z}}^{\dagger}t^{c}U_{\mathbf{z}}-C_{F}\big)

\displaystyle-\mathcal{A}_{0}(\Delta_{\mathrm{UV}})\int_{\mathbf{x}}e^{-i\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\mathbf{x}}C_{F}\big(U_{\mathbf{x}}-1\big)\Bigg]\;,

and:

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathrm{SE3,sub}}^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=-\alpha_{s}\frac{q^{+}\mathbf{q}^{\bar{\lambda}}}{p_{0}^{+}\mathbf{q}^{2}+p_{1}^{+}M^{2}}\mathrm{Dirac}_{\mathrm{LO}}^{\lambda\bar{\lambda}}\int_{k_{\mathrm{min}}^{+}}^{p_{1}^{+}}\frac{\mathrm{d}k^{+}}{k^{+}}\Big(\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\Big)^{2}\Big(\big(1-\frac{2p_{1}^{+}}{k^{+}}\big)^{2}+1\Big)

(69)

\displaystyle\times\Bigg[\int_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{z}}A^{i}(\mathbf{z}-\mathbf{x})A^{i}(\mathbf{z}-\mathbf{x},\Delta_{\mathrm{q}})e^{-i\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\Big(\frac{p_{1}^{+}-k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{x}+\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{z}\Big)}\big(t^{c}U_{\mathbf{x}}U_{\mathbf{z}}^{\dagger}t^{c}U_{\mathbf{z}}-C_{F}\big)

\displaystyle-\mathcal{A}_{0}(\Delta_{\mathrm{UV}})\int_{\mathbf{x}}e^{-i\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\mathbf{x}}C_{F}\big(U_{\mathbf{x}}-1\big)\Bigg]\;.

Diagram SE4

Evaluating the Feynman diagrams corresponding to a quark self-energy
correction after the photon emission, but before the scattering off
the shockwave (see fig.
2
), we find:

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathrm{SE4}}^{0}

\displaystyle=\alpha_{s}C_{F}\frac{p_{0}^{+}\mathbf{q}^{2}-p_{1}^{+}M^{2}}{p_{0}^{+}\mathbf{q}^{2}+p_{1}^{+}M^{2}}\frac{1}{M}\int_{k_{\mathrm{min}}^{+}}^{p_{1}^{+}}\frac{\mathrm{d}k^{+}}{k^{+}}\Big(\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\Big)^{2}\Big(\big(1-\frac{2p_{1}^{+}}{k^{+}}\big)^{2}+D-3\Big)

(70)

\displaystyle\times\mathcal{A}_{0}(\Delta_{\mathrm{q}})\int_{\mathbf{x}}e^{-i\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\mathbf{x}}\big(U_{\mathbf{x}}-1\big)\;,

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathrm{SE4}}^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\!\alpha_{s}C_{F}\frac{q^{+}\mathbf{q}^{\bar{\lambda}}}{p_{0}^{+}\mathbf{q}^{2}\!+\!p_{1}^{+}M^{2}}\mathrm{Dirac}^{\lambda\bar{\lambda}}\big(1\!+\!2\frac{p_{1}^{+}}{q^{+}}\big)\!\int_{k_{\mathrm{min}}^{+}}^{p_{1}^{+}}\frac{\mathrm{d}k^{+}}{k^{+}}\!\Big(\!\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\!\Big)^{2}\!\Big(\!\big(\!1\!-\!\frac{2p_{1}^{+}}{k^{+}}\!\big)^{2}\!+\!D\!-\!3\Big)

\displaystyle\times\mathcal{A}_{0}(\Delta_{\mathrm{q}})\int_{\mathbf{x}}e^{-i\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\mathbf{x}}\big(U_{\mathbf{x}}-1\big)\;.

The counterterms are simply defined by setting
\mathcal{A}_{0}(\Delta_{\mathrm{q}})\to\mathcal{A}_{0}(\Delta_{\mathrm{UV}})
,
and are once again opposite to those of
\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathrm{SE3}}^{0,\lambda}
:

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathrm{SE4,UV}}^{0,\lambda}

\displaystyle=-\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathrm{SE3,UV}}^{0,\lambda}\;.

(71)

In
D=4
dimensions, the subtracted amplitudes are then given by:

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathrm{SE4,sub}}^{0,\lambda}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathrm{LO2}}^{0,\lambda}\frac{\alpha_{s}C_{F}}{4\pi}\int_{k_{\mathrm{min}}^{+}}^{p_{1}^{+}}\frac{\mathrm{d}k^{+}}{k^{+}}\Big(\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\Big)^{2}\Big(\big(1-\frac{2p_{1}^{+}}{k^{+}}\big)^{2}+1\Big)\ln\frac{\Delta_{\mathrm{q}}}{\Delta_{\mathrm{UV}}}\;.

(72)

3.2
Vertex corrections

Figure 3:
The four virtual contributions with a vertex correction,
which in two cases (
\mathrm{V2}
and
\mathrm{V3}
) scatters off
the shockwave.

Diagram V1

The amplitude for Feynman diagram V1 (see fig.
3
) reads,
when the produced virtual photon is longitudinally polarized:

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathrm{V1}}^{0}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\alpha_{s}C_{F}}{p_{0}^{+}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{2}+p_{1}^{+}M^{2}}\frac{1}{M}\frac{1}{D-2}\int_{k_{\mathrm{min}}^{+}}^{p_{1}^{+}}\frac{\mathrm{d}k^{+}}{k^{+}}\frac{(k^{+}q^{+})^{2}}{p_{0}^{+}(p_{1}^{+}-k^{+})(p_{0}^{+}-k^{+})}\mathrm{Dirac}_{\mathrm{V}}^{j0j}

(73)

\displaystyle\times\Bigg\{\Bigg[-\Delta_{\mathrm{P}}+\frac{(p_{0}^{+}-k^{+})(p_{1}^{+}-k^{+})}{p_{1}^{+}(q^{+})^{2}}\big(p_{0}^{+}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{2}-p_{1}^{+}M^{2}\big)\Bigg]\mathcal{A}_{0}(\Delta_{\mathrm{P}})

\displaystyle-2\hat{M}^{2}\Big(\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\Big)^{2}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{2}\mathcal{B}_{1}\big(0,\Delta_{\mathrm{P}},\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}\big)\Bigg\}\int_{\mathbf{x}}e^{-i\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\mathbf{x}}\big(U_{\mathbf{x}}-1\big)\;,

with the Dirac structure (simplified using the spinor relations in
sec.
B
):

\displaystyle\mathrm{Dirac}_{\mathrm{V}}^{\bar{\eta}0\eta^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\equiv\Big[\big(1-2\frac{p_{1}^{+}}{k^{+}}\big)\delta^{\eta\bar{\eta}}\mathds{1}_{4}-i\sigma^{\eta\bar{\eta}}\Big]\Big[\big(2\frac{p_{0}^{+}}{k^{+}}-1\big)\delta^{\eta\eta^{\prime}}\mathds{1}_{4}-i\sigma^{\eta\eta^{\prime}}\Big]\;,

(74)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Dirac}_{\mathrm{V}}^{j0j}

\displaystyle=-(D-2)\Big(\big(2\frac{p_{1}^{+}}{k^{+}}-1\big)\big(2\frac{p_{0}^{+}}{k^{+}}-1\big)+D-3\Big)\;,

and the definition:

\hat{M}^{2}\equiv\frac{(p_{1}^{+}-k^{+})(p_{0}^{+}-k^{+})}{(q^{+})^{2}}M^{2}+i0^{+}\;.

(75)

Expression (
73
) features the UV-divergent integral

\mathcal{A}_{0}(\Delta_{\mathrm{P}})
, as well as the integral
\mathcal{B}_{1}

whose expression is a finite albeit complicated sum of hypergeometric
functions, see eqs. (
408
) and (
412
)
in the appendix.

When the virtual photon is transversely polarized, the amplitude reads:

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathrm{V1}}^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\alpha_{s}C_{F}}{p_{0}^{+}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{2}+p_{1}^{+}M^{2}}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{\eta^{\prime}}\frac{1}{D-2}\int_{k_{\mathrm{min}}^{+}}^{p_{1}^{+}}\frac{\mathrm{d}k^{+}}{k^{+}}\frac{(k^{+}q^{+})^{2}}{p_{0}^{+}(p_{1}^{+}-k^{+})(p_{0}^{+}-k^{+})}\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}

(76)

\displaystyle\times\Bigg\{\Bigg[\Big(\frac{1}{2}-p_{1}^{+}\frac{p_{0}^{+}-k^{+}}{q^{+}k^{+}}\Big)\delta^{\bar{\lambda}\eta^{\prime}}\mathrm{Dirac}_{\mathrm{V}}^{j\bar{\lambda}j}+\epsilon^{ij}\mathrm{Dirac}_{\mathrm{V}}^{i\bar{\lambda}j}\frac{\epsilon^{\bar{\lambda}\eta^{\prime}}}{D-3}\frac{1}{2}\Bigg]\mathcal{A}_{0}(\Delta_{\mathrm{P}})

\displaystyle+\Bigg[-\Bigg(\frac{p_{0}^{+}(p_{1}^{+}-k^{+})}{k^{+}q^{+}}\Big(\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\Big)^{2}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\Big(\Delta_{\mathrm{P}}+\Big(\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\Big)^{2}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{2}\Big)\Bigg)\delta^{\bar{\lambda}\eta^{\prime}}\mathrm{Dirac}_{\mathrm{V}}^{j\bar{\lambda}j}

\displaystyle+\epsilon^{ij}\mathrm{Dirac}_{\mathrm{V}}^{i\bar{\lambda}j}\frac{\epsilon^{\bar{\lambda}\eta^{\prime}}}{D-3}\frac{1}{2}\Big(\Delta_{\mathrm{P}}+\Big(\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\Big)^{2}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{2}\Big)\Bigg]\mathcal{B}_{1}\big(0,\Delta_{\mathrm{P}},\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}\big)\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\times\int_{\mathbf{x}}e^{-i\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\mathbf{x}}\big(U_{\mathbf{x}}-1\big)\;,

with:

\displaystyle\mathrm{Dirac}_{\mathrm{V}}^{\bar{\eta}\bar{\lambda}\eta^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\Big[\big(1-2\frac{p_{1}^{+}}{k^{+}}\big)\delta^{\eta\bar{\eta}}\mathds{1}_{4}-i\sigma^{\eta\bar{\eta}}\Big]\Big[\big(1-2\frac{k^{+}-p_{1}^{+}}{q^{+}}\big)\delta^{\lambda\bar{\lambda}}\mathds{1}_{4}-i\sigma^{\lambda\bar{\lambda}}\Big]

(77)

\displaystyle\times\Big[\big(2\frac{p_{0}^{+}}{k^{+}}-1\big)\delta^{\eta\eta^{\prime}}\mathds{1}_{4}-i\sigma^{\eta\eta^{\prime}}\Big]\;,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Dirac}_{\mathrm{V}}^{j\bar{\lambda}j}

\displaystyle=(D-2)\big(1-2\frac{k^{+}-p_{1}^{+}}{q^{+}}\big)\Big(\big(1-2\frac{p_{1}^{+}}{k^{+}}\big)\big(2\frac{p_{0}^{+}}{k^{+}}-1\big)-(D-3)\Big)\delta^{\lambda\bar{\lambda}}\mathds{1}_{4}

\displaystyle-i\Bigg[(D-2)\Big(\big(1-2\frac{p_{1}^{+}}{k^{+}}\big)\big(2\frac{p_{0}^{+}}{k^{+}}-1\big)-(D-3)\Big)+8(D-4\big)\Bigg]\sigma^{\lambda\bar{\lambda}}\;.

We introduce the following counterterms to absorb the UV divergences:

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathrm{V1,\mathrm{UV}}}^{0}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\alpha_{s}C_{F}}{p_{0}^{+}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{2}+p_{1}^{+}M^{2}}\frac{1}{M}\frac{1}{D-2}\int_{k_{\mathrm{min}}^{+}}^{p_{1}^{+}}\frac{\mathrm{d}k^{+}}{k^{+}}\frac{(k^{+}q^{+})^{2}}{p_{0}^{+}(p_{1}^{+}-k^{+})(p_{0}^{+}-k^{+})}\mathrm{Dirac}_{\mathrm{V}}^{j0j}

(78)

\displaystyle\times\!\Bigg[\!-\!\Delta_{\mathrm{P}}\!+\!\frac{(p_{0}^{+}\!-\!k^{+})(p_{1}^{+}\!-\!k^{+})}{p_{1}^{+}(q^{+})^{2}}\big(p_{0}^{+}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{2}\!-\!p_{1}^{+}M^{2}\big)\!\Bigg]\!\mathcal{A}_{0}(\Delta_{\mathrm{UV}})\!\int_{\mathbf{x}}e^{-i\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\mathbf{x}}\!\big(\!U_{\mathbf{x}}\!-\!1\!\big)\!\,,

and

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathrm{V1,UV}}^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\alpha_{s}C_{F}}{p_{0}^{+}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{2}+p_{1}^{+}M^{2}}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{\eta^{\prime}}\frac{1}{D-2}\int_{k_{\mathrm{min}}^{+}}^{p_{1}^{+}}\frac{\mathrm{d}k^{+}}{k^{+}}\frac{(k^{+}q^{+})^{2}}{p_{0}^{+}(p_{1}^{+}-k^{+})(p_{0}^{+}-k^{+})}\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}

(79)

\displaystyle\times\Bigg[\Big(\frac{1}{2}-p_{1}^{+}\frac{p_{0}^{+}-k^{+}}{q^{+}k^{+}}\Big)\delta^{\bar{\lambda}\eta^{\prime}}\mathrm{Dirac}_{\mathrm{V}}^{j\bar{\lambda}j}+\epsilon^{ij}\mathrm{Dirac}_{\mathrm{V}}^{i\bar{\lambda}j}\frac{\epsilon^{\bar{\lambda}\eta^{\prime}}}{D-3}\frac{1}{2}\Bigg]\mathcal{A}_{0}(\Delta_{\mathrm{UV}})

\displaystyle\times\int_{\mathbf{x}}e^{-i\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\mathbf{x}}\big(U_{\mathbf{x}}-1\big)\;.

The subtracted amplitudes, therefore, become:

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathrm{V1,sub}}^{0}

\displaystyle=\!\frac{\alpha_{s}C_{F}}{p_{0}^{+}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{2}\!+\!p_{1}^{+}M^{2}}\!\frac{1}{M}\!\int_{k_{\mathrm{min}}^{+}}^{p_{1}^{+}}\!\frac{\mathrm{d}k^{+}}{k^{+}}\!\frac{(k^{+}q^{+})^{2}}{p_{0}^{+}(p_{1}^{+}\!-\!k^{+})(p_{0}^{+}\!-\!k^{+})}\!\Big(\!\big(2\frac{p_{1}^{+}}{k^{+}}-\!1\!\big)\!\big(2\frac{p_{0}^{+}}{k^{+}}\!-\!1\big)\!+\!1\!\Big)\!

(80)

\displaystyle\times\Bigg\{\Bigg[-\Delta_{\mathrm{P}}+\frac{(p_{0}^{+}-k^{+})(p_{1}^{+}-k^{+})}{p_{1}^{+}(q^{+})^{2}}\big(p_{0}^{+}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{2}-p_{1}^{+}M^{2}\big)\Bigg]\frac{1}{4\pi}\ln\frac{\Delta_{\mathrm{UV}}}{\Delta_{\mathrm{P}}}

\displaystyle-2\hat{M}^{2}\Big(\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\Big)^{2}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{2}\mathcal{B}_{1}\big(0,\Delta_{\mathrm{P}},\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}\big)\Bigg\}\int_{\mathbf{x}}e^{-i\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\mathbf{x}}\big(U_{\mathbf{x}}-1\big)\;,

and:

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathrm{V1,sub}}^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\alpha_{s}C_{F}}{p_{0}^{+}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{2}+p_{1}^{+}M^{2}}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{\eta^{\prime}}\frac{1}{D-2}\int_{k_{\mathrm{min}}^{+}}^{p_{1}^{+}}\frac{\mathrm{d}k^{+}}{k^{+}}\frac{(k^{+}q^{+})^{2}}{p_{0}^{+}(p_{1}^{+}-k^{+})(p_{0}^{+}-k^{+})}\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}

(81)

\displaystyle\times\Bigg\{\Bigg[\Big(\frac{1}{2}-p_{1}^{+}\frac{p_{0}^{+}-k^{+}}{q^{+}k^{+}}\Big)\delta^{\bar{\lambda}\eta^{\prime}}\mathrm{Dirac}_{\mathrm{V}}^{j\bar{\lambda}j}+\epsilon^{ij}\mathrm{Dirac}_{\mathrm{V}}^{i\bar{\lambda}j}\frac{\epsilon^{\bar{\lambda}\eta^{\prime}}}{D-3}\frac{1}{2}\Bigg]\frac{1}{4\pi}\ln\frac{\Delta_{\mathrm{UV}}}{\Delta_{\mathrm{P}}}

\displaystyle+\Bigg[-\Bigg(\frac{p_{0}^{+}(p_{1}^{+}-k^{+})}{k^{+}q^{+}}\Big(\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\Big)^{2}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\Big(\Delta_{\mathrm{P}}+\Big(\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\Big)^{2}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{2}\Big)\Bigg)\delta^{\bar{\lambda}\eta^{\prime}}\mathrm{Dirac}_{\mathrm{V}}^{j\bar{\lambda}j}

\displaystyle+\epsilon^{ij}\mathrm{Dirac}_{\mathrm{V}}^{i\bar{\lambda}j}\frac{\epsilon^{\bar{\lambda}\eta^{\prime}}}{D-3}\frac{1}{2}\Big(\Delta_{\mathrm{P}}+\Big(\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\Big)^{2}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{2}\Big)\Bigg]\mathcal{B}_{1}\big(0,\Delta_{\mathrm{P}},\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}\big)\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\times\int_{\mathbf{x}}e^{-i\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\mathbf{x}}\big(U_{\mathbf{x}}-1\big)\;.

Diagram V2

The amplitude for Feynman diagram V2 (see fig.
3
) reads,
in case of a longitudinally polarized virtual photon:

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathrm{V2}}^{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha_{s}}{M}\int_{k_{\mathrm{min}}^{+}}^{p_{1}^{+}}\frac{\mathrm{d}k^{+}}{k^{+}}\frac{(k^{+})^{3}q^{+}}{(p_{0}^{+})^{2}p_{1}^{+}(p_{1}^{+}-k^{+})}\mathrm{Dirac}_{\mathrm{V}}^{\bar{\eta}0\eta^{\prime}}

(82)

\displaystyle\times\int_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{z}}\int_{\mathbf{k}_{1}}e^{-i\mathbf{k}_{1}\cdot(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{z})}\frac{\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\eta^{\prime}}}{\mathbf{k}_{1}^{2}}\int_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}\frac{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\bar{\eta}}}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}}\frac{e^{i\big(\boldsymbol{\ell}+\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}\big)\cdot(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{z})}}{\big(\boldsymbol{\ell}+\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}\big)^{2}+\Delta_{\mathrm{P}}}

\displaystyle\times\Bigg[\big(\boldsymbol{\ell}-\frac{p_{0}^{+}(p_{1}^{+}-k^{+})}{q^{+}p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}\big)^{2}-\frac{(p_{1}^{+}-k^{+})(p_{0}^{+}-k^{+})}{(q^{+})^{2}}M^{2}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\times e^{-i\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\Big(\frac{p_{0}^{+}-k^{+}}{p_{0}^{+}}\mathbf{x}+\frac{k^{+}}{p_{0}^{+}}\mathbf{z}\Big)}[U_{\mathbf{z}}t^{c}U_{\mathbf{z}}^{\dagger}U_{\mathbf{x}}t^{c}-C_{F}]\;.

To the best of our knowledge, the transverse integral over
\boldsymbol{\ell}

does not allow a general analytic solution.
2
2

2

See
Caucal:2021ent
;
Taels:2022tza
for very similar integrals
in NLO
\gamma^{(*)}+A\to\mathrm{dijet}+X
calculations.
However, we expect amplitude
\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathrm{V2}}^{0}

to contain a UV divergence in the limit
\mathbf{z}\to\mathbf{x}
.
With the same procedure as for the counterterms in sec.
3.1
,
namely setting
\mathbf{z}=\mathbf{x}
in the phase and the Wilson-line
structures, but not within the divergent integrations, the integral
over
\mathbf{z}
can be carried out, after which one obtains:

\displaystyle\lim_{\mathbf{z}\to\mathbf{x}}\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathrm{V2}}^{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha_{s}C_{F}}{M}\frac{1}{D-2}\int_{k_{\mathrm{min}}^{+}}^{p_{1}^{+}}\frac{\mathrm{d}k^{+}}{k^{+}}\frac{(k^{+})^{3}q^{+}}{(p_{0}^{+})^{2}p_{1}^{+}(p_{1}^{+}-k^{+})}\mathrm{Dirac}_{\mathrm{V}}^{j0j}

(83)

\displaystyle\times\int_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}\frac{\boldsymbol{\ell}\cdot\big(\boldsymbol{\ell}+\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}\big)}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}\big(\boldsymbol{\ell}+\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}\big)^{2}}\frac{1}{\big(\boldsymbol{\ell}+\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}\big)^{2}+\Delta_{\mathrm{P}}}

\displaystyle\times\Bigg[\big(\boldsymbol{\ell}-\frac{p_{0}^{+}(p_{1}^{+}-k^{+})}{q^{+}p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}\big)^{2}-\frac{(p_{1}^{+}-k^{+})(p_{0}^{+}-k^{+})}{(q^{+})^{2}}M^{2}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\times\int_{\mathbf{x}}e^{-i\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\mathbf{x}}\big(U_{\mathbf{x}}-1\big)\;.

The loop integral can now be evaluated with the help of the identities
(
413
):

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{V2}}^{0}

\displaystyle=\int_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}\frac{\boldsymbol{\ell}\cdot\big(\boldsymbol{\ell}+\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}\big)}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}\big(\boldsymbol{\ell}+\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}\big)^{2}}\frac{1}{\big(\boldsymbol{\ell}+\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}\big)^{2}+\Delta_{\mathrm{P}}}

(84)

\displaystyle\times\Bigg[\big(\boldsymbol{\ell}-\frac{p_{0}^{+}(p_{1}^{+}-k^{+})}{q^{+}p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}\big)^{2}-\frac{(p_{1}^{+}-k^{+})(p_{0}^{+}-k^{+})}{(q^{+})^{2}}M^{2}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{A}_{0}(\Delta_{\mathrm{P}})-p_{0}^{+}\frac{p_{1}^{+}-k^{+}}{k^{+}q^{+}}\Big(\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\Big)^{2}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{2}\mathcal{B}_{1}\big(0,\Delta_{\mathrm{P}},-\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}\big)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\frac{(p_{1}^{+}-k^{+})(p_{0}^{+}-k^{+})}{(q^{+})^{2}p_{1}^{+}}\Big(p_{0}^{+}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{2}-p_{1}^{+}M^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\times\Bigg[\mathcal{B}_{0}(\Delta_{\mathrm{P}})+\mathcal{B}_{0}\big(\Delta_{\mathrm{P}},-\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}\big)-\Big(\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\Big)^{2}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{2}\mathcal{C}_{0}\big(\Delta_{\mathrm{P}},-\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}\big)\Big)\Bigg]\;.

The infrared poles contained in the structures
\mathcal{B}_{0}(\Delta_{\mathrm{P}})
,

\mathcal{B}_{0}\big(0,\Delta_{\mathrm{P}},-\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}\big)
,
and
\mathcal{C}_{0}\big(\Delta_{\mathrm{P}},-\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}\big)

all cancel (see eqs. (
411
) and (
415
)).
Since
\mathcal{B}_{1}\big(0,\Delta_{\mathrm{P}},-\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}\big)

is finite, the only remaining singularity is the ultraviolet one contained
in
\mathcal{A}_{0}(\Delta_{\mathrm{P}})
. We can, therefore, define the counterterm

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathrm{V2,UV}}^{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha_{s}C_{F}}{M}\frac{1}{D-2}\int_{k_{\mathrm{min}}^{+}}^{p_{1}^{+}}\frac{\mathrm{d}k^{+}}{k^{+}}\frac{(k^{+})^{3}q^{+}}{(p_{0}^{+})^{2}p_{1}^{+}(p_{1}^{+}-k^{+})}\mathrm{Dirac}_{\mathrm{V}}^{j0j}

(85)

\displaystyle\times\mathcal{A}_{0}(\Delta_{\mathrm{UV}})\int_{\mathbf{x}}e^{-i\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\mathbf{x}}\big(U_{\mathbf{x}}-1\big)\;.

Finally, the subtracted longitudinal amplitude reads

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathrm{V2,sub}}^{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha_{s}}{M}\int_{k_{\mathrm{min}}^{+}}^{p_{1}^{+}}\frac{\mathrm{d}k^{+}}{k^{+}}\frac{(k^{+})^{3}q^{+}}{(p_{0}^{+})^{2}p_{1}^{+}(p_{1}^{+}-k^{+})}\mathrm{Dirac}_{\mathrm{V}}^{\bar{\eta}0\eta^{\prime}}

(86)

\displaystyle\times\Bigg[\int_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{z}}iA^{\eta^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{z})\int_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}\frac{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\bar{\eta}}}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}}\frac{e^{i\big(\boldsymbol{\ell}+\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}\big)\cdot(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{z})}}{\big(\boldsymbol{\ell}+\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}\big)^{2}+\Delta_{\mathrm{P}}}

\displaystyle\times\Big(\big(\boldsymbol{\ell}-\frac{p_{0}^{+}(p_{1}^{+}-k^{+})}{q^{+}p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}\big)^{2}-\frac{(p_{1}^{+}-k^{+})(p_{0}^{+}-k^{+})}{(q^{+})^{2}}M^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\times e^{-i\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\Big(\frac{p_{0}^{+}-k^{+}}{p_{0}^{+}}\mathbf{x}+\frac{k^{+}}{p_{0}^{+}}\mathbf{z}\Big)}[U_{\mathbf{z}}t^{c}U_{\mathbf{z}}^{\dagger}U_{\mathbf{x}}t^{c}-C_{F}]

\displaystyle-\frac{\delta^{\bar{\eta}\eta^{\prime}}}{2}\mathcal{A}_{0}(\Delta_{\mathrm{UV}})\int_{\mathbf{x}}e^{-i\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\mathbf{x}}C_{F}\big(U_{\mathbf{x}}-1\big)\Bigg]\;.

The amplitude for the emission of a transversely polarized virtual
photon is equal to:

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathrm{V2}}^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\alpha_{s}\int_{k_{\mathrm{min}}^{+}}^{p_{1}^{+}}\frac{\mathrm{d}k^{+}}{k^{+}}\frac{(k^{+})^{3}q^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}(p_{0}^{+})^{2}(p_{1}^{+}-k^{+})}\mathrm{Dirac}_{\mathrm{V}}^{\bar{\eta}\bar{\lambda}\eta^{\prime}}

(87)

\displaystyle\times\int_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{z}}iA^{\eta^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{z})\int_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}e^{i\boldsymbol{\ell}\cdot(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{z})}\frac{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\bar{\eta}}-\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{\bar{\eta}}}{\big(\boldsymbol{\ell}-\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}\big)^{2}}\frac{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\bar{\lambda}}-\frac{p_{0}^{+}-k^{+}}{q^{+}}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{\bar{\lambda}}}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}+\Delta_{\mathrm{P}}}

\displaystyle\times e^{-i\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\Big(\frac{p_{0}^{+}-k^{+}}{p_{0}^{+}}\mathbf{x}+\frac{k^{+}}{p_{0}^{+}}\mathbf{z}\Big)}[U_{\mathbf{z}}t^{c}U_{\mathbf{z}}^{\dagger}U_{\mathbf{x}}t^{c}-C_{F}]\;.

Like in the longitudinal case (
82
), we are not aware of
an analytic solution for the integral over
\boldsymbol{\ell}
. The
integral, however, can be shown to be free from UV divergences (see
appendix
D
.

Diagram V3

We obtain for diagram V3 (see fig.
3
):

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathrm{V3}}^{0}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\alpha_{s}}{M}\int_{k_{\mathrm{min}}^{+}}^{p_{1}^{+}}\frac{\mathrm{d}k^{+}}{k^{+}}\frac{(k^{+})^{3}q^{+}}{(p_{1}^{+})^{2}p_{0}^{+}(p_{0}^{+}-k^{+})}\mathrm{Dirac}_{\mathrm{V}}^{\bar{\eta}0\eta^{\prime}}

(88)

\displaystyle\times\int_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{z}}iA^{\bar{\eta}}(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{z})\int_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\ell}}}\frac{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\eta^{\prime}}}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}}\frac{e^{i\big(\boldsymbol{\ell}+\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{q}\big)\cdot(\mathbf{z}-\mathbf{x})}}{\big(\boldsymbol{\ell}+\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{q}\big)^{2}+\Delta_{\mathrm{q}}}

\displaystyle\times\Bigg[\big(\boldsymbol{\ell}+\frac{p_{0}^{+}-k^{+}}{q^{+}}\mathbf{q}\big)^{2}-\frac{(p_{1}^{+}-k^{+})(p_{0}^{+}-k^{+})}{(q^{+})^{2}}M^{2}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\times e^{-i\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\Big(\frac{p_{1}^{+}-k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{x}+\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{z}\Big)}[U_{\mathbf{z}}t^{c}U_{\mathbf{z}}^{\dagger}U_{\mathbf{x}}t^{c}-C_{F}]\;.

Similarly to the amplitude
\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathrm{V2}}^{0}
,
eq. (
88
) contains a UV divergence in the limit
\mathbf{z}\to\mathbf{x}
:

\displaystyle\lim_{\mathbf{z}\to\mathbf{x}}\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathrm{V3}}^{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha_{s}C_{F}}{M}\frac{1}{D-2}\int_{k_{\mathrm{min}}^{+}}^{p_{1}^{+}}\frac{\mathrm{d}k^{+}}{k^{+}}\frac{(k^{+})^{3}q^{+}}{(p_{1}^{+})^{2}p_{0}^{+}(p_{0}^{+}-k^{+})}\mathrm{Dirac}_{\mathrm{V}}^{j0j}

(89)

\displaystyle\times\int_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\ell}}}\frac{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\ell}}\cdot\big(\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\ell}}+\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{q}\big)}{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\ell}}^{2}\big(\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\ell}}+\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{q}\big)^{2}}\frac{1}{\big(\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\ell}}+\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{q}\big)^{2}+\Delta_{\mathrm{q}}}

\displaystyle\times\Bigg[\big(\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\ell}}+\frac{p_{0}^{+}-k^{+}}{q^{+}}\mathbf{q}\big)^{2}-\frac{(p_{1}^{+}-k^{+})(p_{0}^{+}-k^{+})}{(q^{+})^{2}}M^{2}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\times\int_{\mathbf{x}}e^{-i\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\mathbf{x}}\big(U_{\mathbf{x}}-1\big)\;.

The integral over
\boldsymbol{\ell}
can be calculated with the
help of the identities
408
and (
413
)
in the appendix:

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{V3}}^{0}

\displaystyle=\int_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\ell}}}\frac{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\ell}}\cdot\big(\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\ell}}+\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{q}\big)}{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\ell}}^{2}\big(\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\ell}}+\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{q}\big)^{2}}\frac{1}{\big(\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\ell}}+\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{q}\big)^{2}+\Delta_{\mathrm{q}}}

(90)

\displaystyle\times\Bigg[\big(\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\ell}}+\frac{p_{0}^{+}-k^{+}}{q^{+}}\mathbf{q}\big)^{2}-\frac{(p_{1}^{+}-k^{+})(p_{0}^{+}-k^{+})}{(q^{+})^{2}}M^{2}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{A}_{0}(\Delta_{\mathrm{q}})+p_{1}^{+}\frac{p_{0}^{+}-k^{+}}{q^{+}k^{+}}\Big(\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\Big)^{2}\mathbf{q}^{2}\mathcal{B}_{1}\big(0,\Delta_{\mathrm{q}},\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{q}\big)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\frac{(p_{1}^{+}-k^{+})(p_{0}^{+}-k^{+})}{(q^{+})^{2}p_{1}^{+}}\Big(p_{0}^{+}\mathbf{q}^{2}-p_{1}^{+}M^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\times\Bigg[\mathcal{B}_{0}(\Delta_{\mathrm{q}})+\mathcal{B}_{0}\big(\Delta_{\mathrm{q}},\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{q}\big)-\Big(\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\Big)^{2}\mathbf{q}^{2}\mathcal{C}_{0}\big(\Delta_{\mathrm{q}},\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{q}\big)\Bigg]\;.

Due to the cancellation of IR divergences in the last line of (
90
),
the only remaining divergence is the UV-one in
\mathcal{A}_{0}(\Delta_{\mathrm{q}})
.
We can, therefore, define the counterterm:

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathrm{V3,UV}}^{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha_{s}C_{F}}{M}\frac{1}{D-2}\int_{k_{\mathrm{min}}^{+}}^{p_{1}^{+}}\frac{\mathrm{d}k^{+}}{k^{+}}\frac{(k^{+})^{3}q^{+}}{(p_{1}^{+})^{2}p_{0}^{+}(p_{0}^{+}-k^{+})}\mathrm{Dirac}_{\mathrm{V}}^{j0j}

(91)

\displaystyle\times\mathcal{A}_{0}(\Delta_{\mathrm{UV}})\int_{\mathbf{x}}e^{-i\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\mathbf{x}}\big(U_{\mathbf{x}}-1\big)\;,

which results in the following UV-subtracted amplitude:
