\displaystyle-p_{1}^{+}\delta^{\bar{\eta}\bar{\lambda}}\mathcal{K}\big(\mathbf{x}^{\prime}-\mathbf{z},\Delta_{\mathrm{IS}}\big)\Bigg(\Big(\frac{1}{p_{1}^{+}+p_{3}^{+}}+\frac{1}{p_{0}^{+}}\Big)\Big(\big(1+\frac{2p_{1}^{+}}{p_{3}^{+}}\big)\big(1+2\frac{p_{1}^{+}+p_{3}^{+}}{q^{+}}\big)-1\Big)

\displaystyle-2\Big(\frac{1}{p_{1}^{+}+p_{3}^{+}}-\frac{1}{p_{0}^{+}}\Big)\Big(\frac{p_{1}^{+}+p_{3}^{+}}{q^{+}}-\frac{p_{1}^{+}}{p_{3}^{+}}\Big)\Bigg)\Bigg]

\displaystyle-4\int_{k_{\mathrm{min}}^{+}}^{k_{f}^{+}}\frac{\mathrm{d}p_{3}^{+}}{p_{3}^{+}}\Big(\big(1+2\frac{p_{1}^{+}}{q^{+}}\big)^{2}+1\Big)\Big(\frac{q^{+}\mathbf{q}}{p_{0}^{+}\mathbf{q}^{2}+p_{1}^{+}M^{2}}\Big)^{2}A^{i}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime}-\mathbf{z})A^{i}(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{z})\Bigg\}\;.

10
Conclusions

We have presented the first next-to-leading order calculation of the cross section for the
p+A\to\gamma^{*}+\mathrm{jet}+X
process within the hybrid dilute-dense formalism. Replacing the electromagnetic coupling constant, the result can be carried over to the production of a Z-boson. The Drell-Yan + jet cross section is obtained trivially by multiplying with the
\gamma^{*}/Z\to\ell^{+}+\ell^{-}
branching ratio, unless angular correlations between both leptons are measured. In that case, the leptons might be sensitive to the interference between the production of a longitudinally or transversally polarized virtual boson, which is not calculated explicitly in this work but can in principle be computed using our intermediate results presented here.

For phenomenological applications, the gluon channel
g+A\to\gamma^{*}+q+\bar{q}
should be included. The representative Feynman diagrams are depicted in figure
10
. Assuming the photon has nonvanishing plus-momentum and virtuality, the amplitudes where the incoming gluon interacts with the shockwave are completely finite, as are the amplitudes with instantaneous
g\to\gamma^{*}q\bar{q}
splittings. However, the remaining amplitudes, corresponding to the diagrams where the quark-antiquark pair scatters off the shockwave before or after emitting the photon, are not. Indeed, integrating over the momentum of the antiquark (quark) will yield a singularity when this antiquark (quark) is collinear to its parent gluon. These contributions are proportional to the leading-order amplitudes for the quark (antiquark) channel, where the incoming quark (antiquark) comes from the
g\to q\bar{q}
splitting in the DGLAP evolution of the PDF. Indeed, these terms were not taken into account in our analysis in section
6
. We leave the gluon channel for future work.

Our result encompasses the NLO cross section for prompt photon plus jet hadroproduction as an important by-product, easily obtained by taking the real photon limit. This process has been studied quite extensively in the literature at leading order
Jalilian-Marian:2012wwi
;
Rezaeian:2012wa
;
Benic:2017znu
;
Goncalves:2020tvh
;
Benic:2022ixp
;
Ganguli:2023joy
and is planned to me measured with the proposed forward calorimeter (FoCal) of the ALICE experiment
ALICE:2020mso
;
ALICE:2023fov
. The next-to-leading cross section we provide will greatly increase the potential of this study.

One of the major motivations behind this work is the prospect of studying the region where the virtual photon and the jet are back-to-back in the transverse plane
Boer:2017xpy
, hence
{\mathbf{P}^{2}_{\perp}\gg\mathbf{k}^{2}_{\perp}}
. It was observed in ref.
Dominguez:2010xd
that in this regime the leading-order cross section factorizes into a convolution of a hard part with the quark PDF and a (dipole-type) gluon TMD PDF
Mulders:2000sh
. Proving such a factorization at next-to-leading order would be much more complex, complicated amongst others by the emergence of large Sudakov logarithms in the ratio
{\mathbf{P}^{2}_{\perp}/\mathbf{k}^{2}_{\perp}}
, which should be absorbed into the Collins-Soper-Sterman
Collins:2011zzd
evolution of the gluon TMD. However, should the above nontrivial scenario indeed take place, it would be a further confirmation of the CGC and TMD factorization being compatible at one loop. First steps in this direction were taken in
Mueller:2012uf
;
Mueller:2013wwa
. In our recent work
Taels:2022tza
we considered the case of dijet photoproduction, and provided the first proof that large Sudakov double logarithms can be extracted in a way consistent with high-energy resummation provided the latter is kinematically improved. In a series of papers
Caucal:2022ulg
;
Caucal:2023nci
;
Caucal:2023fsf
, complete next-to-leading order TMD factorization was then established for back-to-back dijet production in deep-inelastic scattering in the CGC. We should remark that the study of combined low-
x
and Sudakov resummation is a very active field, undertaken within several different formalisms, see e.g.,
Hautmann:2008vd
;
Deak:2009xt
;
Sun:2011iw
;
Deak:2011ga
;
Dooling:2014kia
;
Balitsky:2015qba
;
Marzani:2015oyb
;
Hentschinski:2016wya
;
Bury:2017jxo
;
Xiao:2017yya
;
Hentschinski:2017ayz
;
Deak:2018obv
;
Zhou:2018lfq
;
Stasto:2018rci
;
Blanco:2019qbm
;
Marquet:2019ltn
;
vanHameren:2019ysa
;
Zheng:2019zul
;
vanHameren:2020rqt
;
Hentschinski:2020tbi
;
Balitsky:2020jzt
;
Nefedov:2020ugj
;
Hentschinski:2021lsh
;
Nefedov:2021vvy
;
Boer:2022njw
;
Al-Mashad:2022zbq
;
Shi:2022hee
;
Balitsky:2022vnb
;
vanHameren:2023oiq
;
Balitsky:2023hmh
;
Shi:2023ejp
.

Finally, another interesting future direction is the study of the inclusive Drell-Yan process, obtained from our result by integrating also the outgoing quark out. This integration, albeit nontrivial, should not lead to new divergences. Experimental data on dilepton production at low invariant mass is currently being analyzed by the ATLAS collaboration
Giuli
. Moreover, the intermediate results of our calculation, one could then extend to next-to-leading order the higher-twist analysis of the Drell-Yan structure functions performed in
Motyka:2014lya
;
Motyka:2016lta
;
Brzeminski:2016lwh
.

Figure 10:
The representative Feynman diagrams for the
gluon channel, i.e. the partonic process
g+A\to\gamma^{*}+q+\bar{q}
.
Only the second and third diagram (and its
q\leftrightarrow\bar{q}
counterparts)
contain a singularity, namely when the antiquark (quark) is integrated
out. These contributions can then be viewed as the LO quark channel
amplitudes, where the incoming quark is generated from the
g\to q\bar{q}

splitting of the gluon PDF. Hence, these collinear divergences should
be absorbed into the DGLAP evolution of the LO cross section.

Appendix A
Transverse integral identities

A standard tool for evaluating the different transverse integrals
in our calculation is the Schwinger trick:

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma(\alpha)}{A^{\alpha}}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}t\,t^{\alpha-1}e^{-At}\;,

(399)

using which we find, for instance, the following expressions for the
Weizsäcker-Williams field (taking the derivative with respect
to
\mathbf{x}
):

\displaystyle iA^{i}(\mathbf{x})\equiv\int_{\mathbf{k}}e^{-i\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{x}}\frac{\mathbf{k}^{i}}{\mathbf{k}^{2}}=\frac{-i}{2\pi^{\frac{D}{2}-1}}\frac{\mathbf{x}^{i}}{(\mathbf{x}^{2})^{\frac{D}{2}-1}}\Gamma(\frac{D}{2}-1)\overset{D\to 4}{=}\frac{-i}{2\pi}\frac{\mathbf{x}^{i}}{\mathbf{x}^{2}}\;,

(400)

and its generalization:

\displaystyle iA^{i}(\mathbf{x},\Delta)

\displaystyle\equiv\int_{\mathbf{k}}e^{-i\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{x}}\frac{\mathbf{k}^{i}}{\mathbf{k}^{2}+\Delta}\;,

(401)

\displaystyle=-i\frac{\mu^{4-D}}{(2\pi)^{\frac{D-2}{2}}}(\sqrt{\Delta})^{\frac{D-2}{2}}\frac{\mathbf{x}^{i}}{|\mathbf{x}|^{\frac{D-2}{2}}}\mathrm{K}_{\frac{D-2}{2}}\big(\sqrt{\mathbf{x}^{2}\Delta}\big)\;,

\displaystyle\overset{D\to 4}{=}\frac{-i}{2\pi}\frac{\mathbf{x}^{i}}{|\mathbf{x}|}\sqrt{\Delta}\mathrm{K}_{1}\big(\sqrt{\mathbf{x}^{2}\Delta}\big)\;.

Note that the latter satisfies the following identity, for a constant

\chi
:

\displaystyle iA^{i}(\chi\mathbf{x},\Delta)

\displaystyle=\chi^{3-D}iA^{i}(\mathbf{x},\chi^{2}\Delta)\;.

(402)

A second very useful identity is the following:

\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}s\,s^{\nu-1}e^{-B/s}e^{-Cs}=2\Big(\frac{B}{C}\Big)^{\frac{\nu}{2}}\mathrm{K}_{-\nu}\big(2\sqrt{BC}\big)\;,

(403)

which we can use to solve, e.g.:

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}(\mathbf{x},\Delta)\equiv\int_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}\frac{e^{i\boldsymbol{\ell}\cdot\mathbf{x}}}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}+\Delta}=\frac{\mu^{4-D}}{(2\pi)^{\frac{D-2}{2}}}\Big(\frac{\mathbf{x}^{2}}{\Delta}\Big)^{\frac{4-D}{4}}K_{\frac{D-4}{2}}\big(\sqrt{\mathbf{x}^{2}\Delta}\big)\;.

(404)

The above integral is finite when
\Delta\neq 0
, since
\Delta

acts as an infrared regulator, while the phase cuts off ultraviolet
divergences. We can, therefore, set
D=4
and take the derivative
with respect to
\mathbf{x}
to obtain:

\displaystyle\int_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}e^{i\boldsymbol{\ell}\cdot\mathbf{x}}\frac{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}-\Delta}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}+\Delta}

\displaystyle=-2\Delta\mathcal{K}(\mathbf{x},\Delta)\;.

(405)

The following UV-divergent loop integral is omnipresent in our calculation,
and is very straightforward to solve (see e.g.
Peskin:1995ev
) using
dimensional regularization with
D=4-2\epsilon_{\mathrm{UV}}
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{0}(\Delta)

\displaystyle\equiv\int_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}\frac{1}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}+\Delta}=\frac{1}{4\pi}\Gamma\big(\frac{4-D}{2}\big)\Big(\frac{\Delta}{4\pi\mu^{2}}\Big)^{\frac{D-4}{2}}\;,

(406)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4\pi}\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{UV}}}-\gamma_{E}+\ln\frac{4\pi\mu^{2}}{\Delta}\Big)+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon_{\mathrm{UV}})\;.

Likewise:

\displaystyle\int_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}\frac{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}+\Delta}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\mu^{4-D}}{(4\pi)^{\frac{D-2}{2}}}\Gamma\Big(\frac{4-D}{2}\Big)\Delta^{\frac{D-2}{2}}\;,

(407)

\displaystyle=-\frac{\Delta}{4\pi}\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{UV}}}-\gamma_{E}+\ln\frac{4\pi\mu^{2}}{\Delta}\Big)+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon_{\mathrm{UV}})\;,

\displaystyle=-\Delta\mathcal{A}_{0}(\Delta)\;.

In the virtual amplitudes, we encounter many transverse loop integrals
that require a bit more of machinery to tackle them. This machinery
is provided by the Passarino-Veltman reduction procedure (ref.
Passarino:1978jh
,
see also
Beuf:2016wdz
for a clear outline), using which we
obtain the following identities:

\displaystyle\int_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}\frac{1}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}+\Omega}\frac{1}{\big(\mathbf{\boldsymbol{\ell}}\!+\mathbf{k}\big)^{2}+\Delta}

\displaystyle=\!\mathcal{B}_{0}(\Omega,\Delta,\mathbf{k})\;,

(408)

\displaystyle\int_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}\frac{\mathbf{\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{i}}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}+\Omega}\frac{1}{\big(\mathbf{\boldsymbol{\ell}}+\mathbf{k}\big)^{2}+\Delta}

\displaystyle\equiv\!\mathbf{k}^{i}\mathcal{B}_{1}(\Omega,\Delta,\mathbf{k})\;,

\displaystyle=\!\frac{\mathbf{k}^{i}}{2\mathbf{k}^{2}}\Big(\mathcal{A}_{0}(\Omega)-\mathcal{A}_{0}(\Delta)+(\Omega-\Delta-\mathbf{k}^{2})\mathcal{B}_{0}(\Omega,\Delta,\mathbf{k})\Big)\;,

\displaystyle\int_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}\frac{\mathbf{\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{i}\mathbf{\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{j}}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}+\Omega}\frac{1}{\big(\mathbf{\boldsymbol{\ell}}+\mathbf{k}\big)^{2}+\Delta}

\displaystyle=\!\frac{\mathbf{k}^{i}\mathbf{k}^{j}}{\mathbf{k}^{2}}\frac{1}{D-3}\Big(\frac{D-4}{2}\mathcal{A}_{0}(\Delta)+\Omega\mathcal{B}_{0}(\Omega,\Delta,\mathbf{k})

\displaystyle+\!\frac{D-2}{2}(\Omega-\Delta-\mathbf{k}^{2})\mathcal{B}_{1}(\Omega,\Delta,\mathbf{k})\Big)\!+\!\frac{\delta^{ij}}{D\!-\!3}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{A}_{0}(\Delta)

\displaystyle-\Omega\mathcal{B}_{0}(\Omega,\Delta,\mathbf{k})-\frac{1}{2}(\Omega\!-\!\Delta\!-\!\mathbf{k}^{2})\mathcal{B}_{1}(\Omega,\Delta,\mathbf{k})\Big)\;,

\displaystyle\mathbf{k}^{j}\!\int_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}\frac{\mathbf{\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{i}\mathbf{\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{j}}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}+\Omega}\frac{1}{\big(\mathbf{\boldsymbol{\ell}}+\mathbf{k}\big)^{2}+\Delta}

\displaystyle=\!\frac{\mathbf{k}^{i}}{2}\Big(\mathcal{A}_{0}(\Delta)+(\Omega-\Delta-\mathbf{k}^{2})\mathcal{B}_{1}(\Omega,\Delta,\mathbf{k})\Big)\;,

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{0}(\Delta,\mathbf{k})

\displaystyle\equiv\!\mathcal{B}_{0}(0,\Delta,\mathbf{k})=\mathcal{B}_{0}(\Delta,0,\mathbf{k})\;,

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{0}(\Delta)

\displaystyle\equiv\!\mathcal{B}_{0}(0,\Delta,0)=\mathcal{B}_{0}(\Delta,0,0)\;.

All the above integrals all are reduced to functions of the simple
scalar integrals
\mathcal{A}_{0}(\Delta)
and
\mathcal{B}_{0}(\Omega,\Delta,\mathbf{k})
.
As already explained above,
\mathcal{A}_{0}(\Delta)
is divergent
in the ultraviolet. In the limit
\Delta\to 0
, a second, infrared
divergence appears, which exactly cancels the UV one, at least within
dimensional regularization. Another way to see this is that
\mathcal{A}_{0}(0)

is a scaleless integral:

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{0}(0)

\displaystyle=\int_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}\frac{1}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}}=\frac{1}{4\pi}\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{UV}}}-\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{IR}}}\Big)=0\;.

(409)

The above property plays a central role in the analysis of field-strength
renormalization amplitudes, section
3.6
.

From simple power counting it follows that the integral
\mathcal{B}_{0}(\Omega,\Delta,\mathbf{k})
,
defined in the first line of eq. (
408
),
is divergent in the infrared when one of its first two arguments tends
to zero. Indeed, introducing Feynman parameters (see e.g.
Peskin:1995ev
):

\displaystyle\frac{1}{A_{1}^{m_{1}}A_{2}^{m_{2}}...A_{n}^{m_{n}}}

\displaystyle=\!\int_{0}^{1}\!\mathrm{d}x_{1}...\mathrm{d}x_{n}\delta(\sum x_{i}\!-\!1)\frac{x_{1}^{m_{1}-1}\!\cdot\!x_{2}^{m_{2}-1}\!\cdot\!...\!\cdot\!x_{n}^{m_{n}-1}}{\big(x_{1}A_{1}\!+\!x_{2}A_{2}\!+\!...\!+\!x_{n}A_{n}\big)^{(m_{1}\!+\!m_{2}\!+\!...\!+\!m_{n})}}

(410)

\displaystyle\times\frac{\Gamma(m_{1}+m_{2}+...+m_{n})}{\Gamma(m_{1})\Gamma(m_{2})...\Gamma(m_{n})}\;,

and writing
D=4-2\epsilon_{\mathrm{IR}}
, we obtain in the
\overline{\mathrm{MS}}

scheme (where
\mu^{2}\to\mu^{2}e^{\gamma_{E}}/4\pi
):

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{0}(0,\Delta,\mathbf{k})

\displaystyle=\mu^{4-D}\Big(\frac{1}{4\pi}\Big)^{\frac{D-2}{2}}\Gamma\big(\frac{6-D}{2}\big)\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}x\frac{1}{x^{\frac{6-D}{2}}\big((1-x)\mathbf{k}^{2}+\Delta\big)^{\frac{6-D}{2}}}\;,

(411)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4\pi}\frac{-1}{\mathbf{k}^{2}+\Delta}\Bigg(\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{IR}}}-\ln\frac{\Delta+\mathbf{k}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}-\,_{2}F_{1}^{(0,0,1,0)}\big(0,1,1,\frac{\mathbf{k}^{2}}{\mathbf{k}^{2}+\Delta}\big)

\displaystyle+\,_{2}F_{1}^{(0,1,0,0)}\big(0,1,1,\frac{\mathbf{k}^{2}}{\mathbf{k}^{2}+\Delta}\big)-\,_{2}F_{1}^{(1,0,0,0)}\big(0,1,1,\frac{\mathbf{k}^{2}}{\mathbf{k}^{2}+\Delta}\big)\Bigg)+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon_{\mathrm{IR}})\;,

\displaystyle=\mathcal{B}_{0}(\Delta,0,\mathbf{k})\;,

where
{}_{2}F_{1}{}^{(0,0,1,0)}
is the derivative of the third argument
of the hypergeometric function
{}_{2}F_{1}
. The above calculation
serves to prove that all the other expressions in (
408
)
are finite when either
\Delta
or
\Omega
is zero.
For example:

\displaystyle\lim_{\Omega\to 0}\mathcal{B}_{1}(\Omega,\Delta,\mathbf{k})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\mathbf{k}^{2}}\Big(\mathcal{A}_{0}(0)-\mathcal{A}_{0}(\Delta)+(-\Delta-\mathbf{k}^{2})\mathcal{B}_{0}(0,\Delta,\mathbf{k})\Big)\;,

(412)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\mathbf{k}^{2}}\Big(\mathcal{A}_{0}(0)-\mathcal{A}_{0}(\Delta)+\frac{1}{4\pi}\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{IR}}}\Big)+\mathrm{finite}\;,

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\mathbf{k}^{2}}\frac{1}{4\pi}\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{UV}}}-\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{IR}}}-\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{UV}}}+\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{IR}}}\Big)+\mathrm{finite}\;.

A second useful set of integrals is:

\displaystyle\int_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}\frac{1}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}}\frac{1}{\mathbf{\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{2}\!+\!\Delta}\frac{1}{\big(\mathbf{\boldsymbol{\ell}}\!+\!\mathbf{k}\big)^{2}+\Pi}

\displaystyle=\!\mathcal{C}_{0}(\Delta,\Pi,\mathbf{k})\;,

(413)

\displaystyle\int_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}\frac{\mathbf{\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{i}}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}}\frac{1}{\mathbf{\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{2}\!+\!\Delta}\frac{1}{\big(\mathbf{\boldsymbol{\ell}}\!+\!\mathbf{k}\big)^{2}\!+\!\Pi}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{k}^{i}\mathcal{C}_{1}(\Delta,\Pi,\mathbf{k})\;,

\displaystyle=\!\frac{\mathbf{k}^{i}}{2\mathbf{k}^{2}}\Big(\mathcal{B}_{0}(\Delta)\!-\!\mathcal{B}_{0}(\Delta,\Pi,\mathbf{k})\!-\!(\mathbf{k}^{2}\!+\!\Pi)\mathcal{C}_{0}(\Delta,\Pi,\mathbf{k})\Big)\;,

\displaystyle\mathbf{k}^{j}\!\int_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}\frac{\mathbf{\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{i}\mathbf{\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{j}}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}}\frac{1}{\mathbf{\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{2}\!+\!\Delta}\frac{1}{\big(\mathbf{\boldsymbol{\ell}}\!+\!\mathbf{k}\big)^{2}\!+\!\Pi}

\displaystyle=\!-\frac{\mathbf{k}^{i}}{2}\Big(\mathcal{B}_{1}(\Delta,\Pi,\mathbf{k})\!+\!(\mathbf{k}^{2}\!+\!\Pi)\mathcal{C}_{1}(\Delta,\Pi,\mathbf{k})\Big)\;,

\displaystyle\int_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}\frac{\mathbf{\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{i}\mathbf{\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{j}}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}}\frac{1}{\mathbf{\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{2}\!+\!\Delta}\frac{1}{\big(\mathbf{\boldsymbol{\ell}}\!+\!\mathbf{k}\big)^{2}\!+\!\Pi}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{C}^{ij}(\Delta,\Pi,\mathbf{k})=\mathbf{k}^{i}\mathbf{k}^{j}\mathcal{C}_{21}(\Delta,\Pi,\mathbf{k})\!+\!\delta^{ij}\mathcal{C}_{22}(\Delta,\Pi,\mathbf{k})\;,

with:

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{22}(\Delta,\Pi,\mathbf{k})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{D-3}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{B}_{1}(\Delta,\Pi,\mathbf{k})+\frac{1}{2}(\mathbf{k}^{2}+\Pi)\mathcal{C}_{1}(\Delta,\Pi,\mathbf{k})+\mathcal{B}_{0}(\Delta,\Pi,\mathbf{k})\Big)\;,

(414)

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{21}(\Delta,\Pi,\mathbf{k})

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{D-3}\frac{1}{\mathbf{k}^{2}}\Big(\mathcal{B}_{0}(\Delta,\Pi,\mathbf{k})

\displaystyle+\frac{D-2}{2}\mathcal{B}_{1}(\Delta,\Pi,\mathbf{k})+\frac{D-2}{2}(\mathbf{k}^{2}+\Pi)\mathcal{C}_{1}(\Delta,\Pi,\mathbf{k})\Big)\;.

Explicitly, we find that:

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{0}(\Delta,\mathbf{k})

\displaystyle\equiv\mathcal{C}_{0}(\Delta,0,\mathbf{k})=\mathcal{C}_{0}(0,\Delta,\mathbf{k})\;,

(415)

\displaystyle=\frac{\mu^{4-D}}{(4\pi)^{\frac{D-2}{2}}}\Gamma\big(\frac{8-D}{2}\big)\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}y\int_{0}^{1-y}\mathrm{d}z\frac{1}{\big(y(1-y)\mathbf{k}^{2}+z\Delta\big)^{\frac{8-D}{2}}}\;,

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{\mathbf{k}^{2}\Delta}\Big(1+\frac{\Delta}{\mathbf{k}^{2}+\Delta}\Big)\frac{1}{4\pi\epsilon_{\mathrm{IR}}}+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon_{\mathrm{IR}}^{0})\;.

We conclude this section with a list of the divergent (either ultraviolet or infrared)
parts of transverse integrals commonly encountered in the calculation,
in particular in the evaluation of the virtual vertex corrections
in section
3.2
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{0}(\Delta)

\displaystyle=\int_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}\frac{1}{\mathbf{\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{2}+\Delta}=\frac{1}{4\pi}\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{UV}}}+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{0})\;,

(416)

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{0}(\Delta,\mathbf{k})

\displaystyle=\int_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}\frac{1}{\big(\mathbf{\boldsymbol{\ell}}+\mathbf{k}\big)^{2}}\frac{1}{\mathbf{\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{2}+\Delta}=-\frac{1}{\mathbf{k}^{2}+\Delta}\frac{1}{4\pi\epsilon_{\mathrm{IR}}}+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{0})\;,

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{0}(\Delta,\mathbf{k})

\displaystyle=\int_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}\frac{1}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}}\frac{1}{\big(\mathbf{\boldsymbol{\ell}}+\mathbf{k}\big)^{2}}\frac{1}{\mathbf{\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{2}+\Delta}=\frac{\mathbf{k}^{2}+2\Delta}{\mathbf{k}^{2}\Delta}\mathcal{B}_{0}(\Delta,\mathbf{k})+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{0})\;,

\displaystyle\mathbf{k}^{i}\mathcal{C}_{1}(\Delta,\mathbf{k})

\displaystyle=\int_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}\frac{\mathbf{\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{i}}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}}\frac{1}{\big(\mathbf{\boldsymbol{\ell}}+\mathbf{k}\big)^{2}}\frac{1}{\mathbf{\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{2}+\Delta}=-\frac{\mathbf{k}^{i}}{\mathbf{k}^{2}}\mathcal{B}_{0}(\Delta,\mathbf{k})+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{0})\;,

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}^{ij}(\Delta,\mathbf{k})

\displaystyle=\int_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}\frac{\mathbf{\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{i}\mathbf{\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{j}}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}}\frac{1}{\big(\mathbf{\boldsymbol{\ell}}+\mathbf{k}\big)^{2}}\frac{1}{\mathbf{\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{2}+\Delta}=\frac{\mathbf{k}^{i}\mathbf{k}^{j}}{\mathbf{k}^{2}}\mathcal{B}_{0}(\Delta,\mathbf{k})+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{0})\;.

Appendix B
Dirac algebra

In this section, we remind the reader of some key gamma matrix identities in transverse
D-2
-dimensional Euclidean space.

First, it follows from the definition
\{\gamma^{\mu},\gamma^{\nu}\}=2g^{\mu\nu}\mathds{1}_{4}
that:

\{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{i},{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{j}\}=-2\delta^{ij}\mathds{1}_{4}\;,

(417)

with the trivial corollary:

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{i}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{i}\mathds{1}_{4}

\displaystyle=-(D-2)\mathds{1}_{4}\;.

(418)

Repeated application of (
417
) brings us to the following important identity:

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{i}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{j}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{k}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{l}

\displaystyle={\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{k}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{l}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{i}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{j}+2\delta^{il}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{k}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{j}-2\delta^{ik}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{l}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{j}+2\delta^{jl}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{i}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{k}-2\delta^{jk}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{i}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{l}\;.

(419)

The Dirac sigma in
D-2
dimensions:
\sigma^{ij}=(i/2)[{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{i},{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{j}]
, can then be shown to satisfy the following commutation relation:

\displaystyle\big[\sigma^{ij},\sigma^{kl}\big]

\displaystyle=2i\delta^{il}\sigma^{kj}-2i\delta^{ik}\sigma^{lj}+2i\delta^{jl}\sigma^{ik}-2i\delta^{jk}\sigma^{il}\;,

(420)

while contracting two Dirac sigmas gives:

\displaystyle\sigma^{ij}\sigma^{il}

\displaystyle=(D-3)\delta^{jl}\mathds{1}_{4}+i(D-4)\sigma^{jl}\;.

(421)

The following identities will be useful as well:

\displaystyle\sigma^{\eta\eta^{\prime}}\sigma^{\lambda\bar{\lambda}}\sigma^{\eta\bar{\eta}}

\displaystyle=2i(D-3)\delta^{\lambda\bar{\eta}}\delta^{\eta^{\prime}\bar{\lambda}}-2(D-4)\delta^{\lambda\bar{\eta}}\sigma^{\eta^{\prime}\bar{\lambda}}-2i\sigma^{\lambda\eta^{\prime}}\sigma^{\bar{\eta}\bar{\lambda}}-2i(D-3)\delta^{\bar{\lambda}\bar{\eta}}\delta^{\eta^{\prime}\lambda}

(422)

\displaystyle+2(D-4)\delta^{\bar{\lambda}\bar{\eta}}\sigma^{\eta^{\prime}\lambda}-2i\sigma^{\bar{\lambda}\eta^{\prime}}\sigma^{\lambda\bar{\eta}}+(D-3)\delta^{\eta^{\prime}\bar{\eta}}\sigma^{\lambda\bar{\lambda}}+i(D-4)\sigma^{\eta^{\prime}\bar{\eta}}\sigma^{\lambda\bar{\lambda}}\;,

and:

\displaystyle\sigma^{\eta\bar{\eta}}\sigma^{\lambda\bar{\lambda}}\sigma^{\eta\bar{\eta}}

\displaystyle=\Big[(D-3)(D-2)-8(D-4)\Big]\sigma^{\lambda\bar{\lambda}}\;.

(423)

Dirac traces can be often simplified using the fact that
\gamma^{+}

and
\gamma^{-}
commute with the transverse gamma matrices and thus also with
\sigma^{ij}
, and then applying the completeness relation

\displaystyle u_{G}^{s}(q^{+})\bar{u}_{G}^{s}(q^{+})\gamma^{+}

\displaystyle=2q^{+}\mathcal{P}_{G}\;,

(424)

with
\mathcal{P}_{G}=\gamma^{-}\gamma^{+}/2
the projector on good spinors.

Using the above definitions, together with the cyclic permutation property
of the spinor trace and the fact that one can reverse the order of
gamma matrices inside a trace:

\mathrm{Tr}(\gamma^{\mu}\gamma^{\nu}\gamma^{\rho}...)=\mathrm{Tr}(...\gamma^{\rho}\gamma^{\nu}\gamma^{\mu})\;,

(425)

it is straightforward to establish the identities:

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}\big(\mathcal{P}_{G}\big)

\displaystyle=2\;,

(426)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}\big(\mathcal{P}_{G}\sigma^{ij}\big)

\displaystyle=0\;,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}\big(\mathcal{P}_{G}\sigma^{ij}\sigma^{kl}\big)

\displaystyle=2(\delta^{ik}\delta^{jl}-\delta^{il}\delta^{jk})\overset{D\to 4}{=}2\epsilon^{ij}\epsilon^{kl}\;.

Moreover, using the above relations as well as the commutation relation
(
420
), it is straightforward to prove the following
relations:

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}\Big\{\mathcal{P}_{G}\big(a\delta^{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}+i\sigma^{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}\big)\big(b\delta^{\eta\bar{\eta}}-i\sigma^{\eta\bar{\eta}}\big)\big(c\delta^{\lambda\bar{\lambda}}-i\sigma^{\lambda\bar{\lambda}}\big)\big(d\delta^{\eta\eta^{\prime}}-i\sigma^{\eta\eta^{\prime}}\big)\Big\}

(427)

\displaystyle=2\Big[\big(ac+D-3\big)\big(bd-(D-3)\big)\delta^{\bar{\eta}\eta^{\prime}}\delta^{\bar{\lambda}\lambda^{\prime}}

\displaystyle+\Big((a+c)(b-d)+(D-4)\big(c-a-b-d\big)+(D-4)^{2}\Big)\epsilon^{\bar{\eta}\eta^{\prime}}\epsilon^{\bar{\lambda}\lambda^{\prime}}\Big]\;,

and:

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}\Big\{\mathcal{P}_{G}\big(a\delta^{\eta\eta^{\prime}}+i\sigma^{\eta\eta^{\prime}}\big)\big(b\delta^{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}+i\sigma^{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}\big)\big(c\delta^{\lambda\bar{\lambda}}-i\sigma^{\lambda\bar{\lambda}}\big)\big(d\delta^{\eta\bar{\eta}}-i\sigma^{\eta\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big\}

(428)

\displaystyle=\!2\Big[\big(ad\!+\!D\!-\!3\big)\big(bc\!+\!D\!-\!3\big)\delta^{\bar{\eta}\eta^{\prime}}\delta^{\bar{\lambda}\lambda^{\prime}}\!-\!\big(a\!+\!d\!+\!D\!-\!4\big)\big(b\!+\!c\!-\!(D\!-\!4)\big)\epsilon^{\bar{\eta}\eta^{\prime}}\epsilon^{\bar{\lambda}\lambda^{\prime}}\Big]\;.

Appendix C
Example calculation

Figure 11:
Feynman diagram for the production of a virtual photon
and a quark via a gluon-quark-antiquark loop. The virtual gluon and
quark scatter off the shockwave.

In this section, we describe in full detail the calculation of the
amplitude corresponding to the diagram A2, depicted in figure
11
.
We follow the Bjorken-Kogut-Soper formulation of light-cone perturbation
theory
Bjorken:1970ah
, and approach the CGC in the spirit of the dipole approach

Mueller:1989st
;
Nikolaev:1990ja
. In particular, we draw heavily from the excellent introduction to LCPT applied to
the CGC in the appendix of ref.
Beuf:2016wdz
.

We remind the reader that the shockwave is treated as an
external static potential. In this background field, we then perturbatively calculate the projectile
dynamics, which take place on a much shorter timescale than those
of the ‘frozen’ target. The leading-order and virtual amplitudes

\mathcal{M}
in our calculation are defined as:

\displaystyle\,{}_{f}\big\langle\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{1})\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{*}(\vec{q})\big|\hat{F}-1\big|\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{0})\big\rangle_{i}

\displaystyle=2\pi\delta(p_{0}^{+}-p_{1}^{+}-q^{+})\mathcal{M}\;,

(429)

where
\hat{F}-1
is the external potential evaluated between the Fock states of the incoming quark
and outgoing quark with a virtual photon or vector boson.

The Fock states appearing in eq. (
429
)
are obtained from the perturbative evolution of the asymptotic eigenstates of the free Hamiltonian
\hat{H}_{0}
:

\displaystyle\big|\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{0})\big\rangle_{i}

\displaystyle=\hat{\mathcal{U}}(0,-\infty)\big|\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{0})\big\rangle\;,

(430)

\displaystyle\,{}_{f}\big\langle\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{1})\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{*}(\vec{q})\big|

\displaystyle=\big\langle\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{1})\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{*}(\vec{q})\big|\hat{\mathcal{U}}(+\infty,0)\;,

where
x^{+}=0
is the space-time position of the shockwave.
Next, remember from eq. (
9
) that:

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{U}}(0,-\infty)

\displaystyle\equiv\hat{T}\exp\Bigl(-i\int_{-\infty}^{0}\mathrm{d}x^{+}\hat{\mathcal{H}}(x^{+})\Bigr)\;,

(431)

where the Hamiltonian has the usual time dependence of the interaction picture:

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{H}}(x^{+})

\displaystyle=e^{i\hat{H}_{0}x^{+}}\hat{V}e^{-i\hat{H}_{0}x^{+}}\;.

(432)

We now obtain:

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{U}}(0,-\infty)\big|\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{0})\big\rangle

\displaystyle=\!\big|\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{0})\big\rangle

(433)

\displaystyle-\!i\!\int\mathrm{PS}(\vec{\ell_{1}},\vec{k}_{1})\int_{-\infty}^{0}\mathrm{d}x^{+}\big\langle\mathbf{q}(\vec{\ell}_{1})\mathbf{g}(\vec{k}_{1})\big|e^{i(\ell_{1}^{-}+k^{-}_{1})x^{+}}\hat{V}e^{-ip^{-}_{0}x^{+}}\big|\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{0})\big\rangle

\displaystyle\times\!\big|\mathbf{q}(\vec{\ell}_{1})\mathbf{g}(\vec{k}_{1})\big\rangle+\mathcal{O}(g_{s}^{2})\;,

\displaystyle=\!\big|\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{0})\big\rangle\!+\!\!\!\int\!\!\mathrm{PS}(\vec{\ell}_{1},\vec{k}_{1})\frac{\big\langle\mathbf{q}(\vec{\ell}_{1})\mathbf{g}(\vec{k}_{1})\big|\hat{V}\big|\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{0})\big\rangle}{p_{0}^{-}\!-\!\ell_{1}^{-}\!-\!k^{-}\!+\!i0^{+}}\big|\mathbf{q}(\vec{\ell}_{1})\mathbf{g}(\vec{k}_{1})\big\rangle\!+\!\mathcal{O}(g_{s}^{2})\;.

Likewise, being careful with the time-ordering operator
\hat{T}
:

\displaystyle\big\langle\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{1})\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{*}(\vec{q})\big|\hat{\mathcal{U}}(+\infty,0)=\big\langle\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{1})\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{*}(\vec{q})\big|+\int\mathrm{PS}(\vec{\ell}_{2},\vec{\ell}_{3})\big\langle\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{1})\mathbf{q}(\vec{\ell}_{2})\bar{\mathbf{q}}(\vec{\ell}_{3})\big|

(434)

\displaystyle\times\big\langle\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{1})\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{*}(\vec{q})\big|\exp\Bigl(-i\int_{0}^{+\infty}\mathrm{d}x^{+}\hat{\mathcal{H}}(x^{+})\Bigr)\big|\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{1})\mathbf{q}(\vec{\ell}_{2})\bar{\mathbf{q}}(\vec{\ell}_{3})\big\rangle+(...)\;,

\displaystyle=\big\langle\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{1})\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{*}(\vec{q})\big|+\int\mathrm{PS}(\vec{k}_{2},\vec{\ell}_{2},\vec{\ell}_{3})\big\langle\mathbf{q}(\vec{\ell}_{2})\mathbf{g}(\vec{k}_{2})\big|

\displaystyle\times\big\langle\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{1})\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{*}(\vec{q})\big|\exp\Bigl(-i\int_{0}^{+\infty}\mathrm{d}x^{+}\hat{\mathcal{H}}(x^{+})\Bigr)\big|\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{1})\mathbf{q}(\vec{\ell}_{2})\bar{\mathbf{q}}(\vec{\ell}_{3})\big\rangle

\displaystyle\times\big\langle\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{1})\mathbf{q}(\vec{\ell}_{2})\bar{\mathbf{q}}(\vec{\ell}_{3})\big|\exp\Bigl(-i\int_{0}^{x^{+}}\mathrm{d}y^{+}\hat{\mathcal{H}}(y^{+})\Bigr)\big|\mathbf{q}(\vec{\ell}_{2})\mathbf{g}(\vec{k}_{2})\big\rangle+(...)\;,

\displaystyle=\big\langle\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{1})\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{*}(\vec{q})\big|+\int\mathrm{PS}(\vec{k}_{2},\vec{\ell}_{2},\vec{\ell}_{3})\big\langle\mathbf{q}(\vec{\ell}_{2})\mathbf{g}(\vec{k}_{2})\big|

\displaystyle\times\frac{\big\langle\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{1})\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{*}(\vec{q})\big|\hat{V}\big|\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{1})\mathbf{q}(\vec{\ell}_{2})\bar{\mathbf{q}}(\vec{\ell}_{3})\big\rangle}{q^{-}-\ell_{2}^{-}-\ell_{3}^{-}+i0^{+}}\frac{\big\langle\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{1})\mathbf{q}(\vec{\ell}_{2})\bar{\mathbf{q}}(\vec{\ell}_{3})\big|\hat{V}\big|\mathbf{q}(\vec{\ell}_{2})\mathbf{g}(\vec{k}_{2})\big\rangle}{p_{1}^{+}+q^{-}-\ell_{2}^{-}-k_{2}^{-}+i0^{+}}+(...)\;,

where (…) stands for all other possible diagrams, generated by inserting
different intermediate Fock states.
Combining (
429
) with (
433
) and (
434
),
we obtain:

\displaystyle{}_{f}\big\langle\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{1})\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{*}(\vec{q})\big|\hat{F}-1\big|\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{0})\big\rangle_{i}=\big\langle\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{1})\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{*}(\vec{q})\big|\hat{F}-1\big|\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{0})\big\rangle

(435)

\displaystyle+\int\mathrm{PS}(\vec{k}_{1},\vec{k}_{2},\vec{\ell}_{1},\vec{\ell}_{2},\vec{\ell}_{3})\frac{\big\langle\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{1})\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{*}(\vec{q})\big|\hat{V}\big|\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{1})\mathbf{q}(\vec{\ell}_{2})\bar{\mathbf{q}}(\vec{\ell}_{3})\big\rangle}{q^{-}-\ell_{2}^{-}-\ell_{3}^{-}+i0^{+}}

\displaystyle\times\frac{\big\langle\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{1})\mathbf{q}(\vec{\ell}_{2})\bar{\mathbf{q}}(\vec{\ell}_{3})\big|\hat{V}\big|\mathbf{q}(\vec{\ell}_{2})\mathbf{g}(\vec{k}_{2})\big\rangle}{p_{1}^{+}+q^{-}-\ell_{2}^{-}-k_{2}^{-}+i0^{+}}\frac{\big\langle\mathbf{q}(\vec{\ell}_{1})\mathbf{g}(\vec{k}_{1})\big|\hat{V}\big|\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{0})\big\rangle}{p_{0}^{-}-\ell_{1}^{-}-k^{-}+i0^{+}}

\displaystyle\times\big\langle\mathbf{q}(\vec{\ell}_{2})\mathbf{g}(\vec{k}_{2})\big|\hat{F}-1\big|\mathbf{q}(\vec{\ell}_{1})\mathbf{g}(\vec{k}_{1})\big\rangle+(...)\;.

Neglecting the leading-order part,
there are three different numerators to evaluate. The interaction
terms of the light-cone Hamiltonian for QCD and QED of interest are:

\displaystyle\hat{V}

\displaystyle=\int\mathrm{d}^{D-3}\vec{x}:g_{\mathrm{em}}\bar{\psi}(\vec{x})\cancel{A}(\vec{x})\psi(\vec{x})+g_{s}t^{c}\bar{\psi}(\vec{x})\cancel{A}^{c}(\vec{x})\psi(\vec{x}):\;.

(436)

We then obtain

\displaystyle\big\langle\mathbf{q}(\vec{\ell}_{1})\mathbf{g}(\vec{k}_{1})\big|\hat{V}\big|\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{0})\big\rangle

\displaystyle=\big\langle 0\big|b_{\vec{\ell}_{1}}\,a_{\vec{k}_{1}}\,\hat{V}\,b_{\vec{p}_{0}}^{\dagger}\big|0\big\rangle\;,

(437)

\displaystyle=g_{s}t^{c}\int\mathrm{d}^{D-3}\vec{x}\big\langle 0\big|b_{\vec{\ell}_{1}}a_{\vec{k}_{1}}:\bar{\psi}(\vec{x})\cancel{A}(\vec{x})\psi(\vec{x}):b_{\vec{p}_{0}}^{\dagger}\big|0\big\rangle\;,

\displaystyle=(2\pi)^{D-1}\delta^{(D-1)}\big(\vec{p}_{0}-\vec{\ell}_{1}-\vec{k}_{1}\big)g_{s}t^{c}\bar{u}(\vec{\ell}_{1})\cancel{\epsilon}^{*}(\vec{k}_{2})u(\vec{p}_{0})\;.

Likewise, the other numerators give:

\displaystyle\big\langle\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{1})\mathbf{q}(\vec{\ell}_{2})\bar{\mathbf{q}}(\vec{\ell}_{3})\big|\hat{V}\big|\mathbf{q}(\vec{\ell}_{2})\mathbf{g}(\vec{k}_{2})\big\rangle

\displaystyle=\!(2\pi)^{D\!-\!1}\delta^{(D\!-\!1)}\big(\vec{p}_{1}\!+\!\vec{\ell}_{3}\!-\!\vec{k}_{2}\big)g_{s}t^{c}\bar{u}(\vec{p}_{1})\cancel{\epsilon}(\vec{k}_{2})v(\vec{\ell}_{3})\;,

(438)

\displaystyle\big\langle\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{1})\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{*}(\vec{q})\big|\hat{V}\big|\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{1})\mathbf{q}(\vec{\ell}_{2})\bar{\mathbf{q}}(\vec{\ell}_{3})\big\rangle

\displaystyle=\!-(2\pi)^{D\!-\!1}\delta^{(D\!-\!1)}\big(\vec{\ell}_{2}\!+\!\vec{\ell}_{3}\!-\!\vec{q}\big)g_{\mathrm{em}}\bar{v}(\vec{\ell}_{3})\cancel{\epsilon}^{*}(\vec{q})u(\vec{p}_{1})\;.

The minus sign in the last line comes from the anticommutation of
the fermion field operators. Note that one should be careful to make
sure that
\big\langle\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{1})\mathbf{q}(\vec{\ell}_{2})\bar{\mathbf{q}}(\vec{\ell}_{3})\big|

and
\big|\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{1})\mathbf{q}(\vec{\ell}_{2})\bar{\mathbf{q}}(\vec{\ell}_{3})\big\rangle

are indeed each other’s conjugate.
9
9

9

I am grateful to Guillaume Beuf for pointing me to this possible source
of error, which allowed me to identify and correct a sign mistake.

We will shortly discuss the evaluation of the Dirac algebra, but let
us first turn to the interaction of the projectile with the shockwave,
encoded in the last line of (
435
). With the help of
the (anti-)commutation relations (
18
) we derive the normalization of the Fock states:

\displaystyle\big\langle\mathbf{q}(\vec{\ell}_{2})\mathbf{g}(\vec{k}_{2})\big|\mathbf{q}(\vec{\ell}_{1})\mathbf{g}(\vec{k}_{1})\big\rangle

\displaystyle=\!2k_{1}^{+}(2\pi)^{D\!-\!1}\delta^{(D\!-\!1)}(\vec{k}_{1}\!-\!\vec{k}_{2})2\ell_{1}^{+}(2\pi)^{D\!-\!1}\delta^{(D\!-\!1)}(\vec{\ell}_{1}\!-\!\vec{\ell}_{2})\;.

(439)

Moreover, the CGC dictates that, in the eikonal approximation, the
shockwave is built from Wilson lines, and, therefore, the action of
the external potential becomes:

\displaystyle\big\langle\mathbf{q}(\vec{\ell}_{2})\mathbf{g}(\vec{k}_{2})\big|\hat{F}\big|\mathbf{q}(\vec{\ell}_{1})\mathbf{g}(\vec{k}_{1})\big\rangle

\displaystyle=2k_{1}^{+}2\pi\delta(k_{1}^{+}-k_{2}^{+})2\ell_{1}^{+}2\pi\delta(\ell_{1}^{+}-\ell_{2}^{+})

(440)

\displaystyle\times\int_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{z}}e^{-i\mathbf{x}\cdot(\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2}-\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1})}e^{-i\mathbf{z}\cdot(\mathbf{k}_{2}-\mathbf{k}_{1})}U_{\mathbf{x}}W_{\mathbf{z}}^{c}\;,

where
U
and
W
are Wilson lines and the fundamental and adjoint
representation, respectively. Filling in the implicit color factors and using the Fierz identity:

t^{d}W^{dc}=U^{\dagger}t^{c}U\;,

(441)

equations (
439
) and (
440
) can be conveniently combined into:

\displaystyle\big\langle\mathbf{q}(\vec{\ell}_{2})\mathbf{g}(\vec{k}_{2})\big|\hat{F}-1\big|\mathbf{q}(\vec{\ell}_{1})\mathbf{g}(\vec{k}_{1})\big\rangle

\displaystyle=2k_{1}^{+}2\pi\delta(k_{1}^{+}-k_{2}^{+})2\ell_{1}^{+}2\pi\delta(\ell_{1}^{+}-\ell_{2}^{+})

(442)

\displaystyle\times\int_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{z}}e^{-i\mathbf{x}\cdot(\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2}-\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1})}e^{-i\mathbf{z}\cdot(\mathbf{k}_{2}-\mathbf{k}_{1})}\big(t^{c}U_{\mathbf{x}}U_{\mathbf{z}}^{\dagger}t^{c}U_{\mathbf{z}}-C_{F}\big)\;,

We are now in a position to extract from definition (
429
)
the expression for the amplitude
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{A2}}
, by
plugging the above equation together with (
437
) and
(
438
) into (
435
), and using the delta
functions to eliminate as many intermediate momenta as possible

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{A2}}

\displaystyle=-g_{\mathrm{em}}g_{s}^{2}\int_{p_{1}^{+}}^{p_{0}^{+}}\frac{\mathrm{d}k^{+}}{2\pi 2k^{+}}\frac{1}{2\ell_{2}^{+}2\ell_{3}^{+}}\int_{\mathbf{k}_{1},\mathbf{k}_{2}}\frac{\bar{u}(\vec{p}_{1})\cancel{\epsilon}(\vec{k}_{2})v(\vec{\ell}_{3})}{q^{-}-\ell_{2}^{-}-\ell_{3}^{-}+i0^{+}}

(443)

\displaystyle\times\frac{\bar{v}(\vec{\ell}_{3})\cancel{\epsilon}^{*}(\vec{q})u(\vec{p}_{1})}{p_{1}^{+}+q^{-}-\ell_{2}^{-}-k_{2}^{-}+i0^{+}}\frac{\bar{u}(\vec{\ell}_{1})\cancel{\epsilon}^{*}(\vec{k}_{2})u(\vec{p}_{0})}{p_{0}^{-}-\ell_{1}^{-}-k^{-}+i0^{+}}

\displaystyle\times\int_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{z}}e^{-i\mathbf{x}\cdot(\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2}-\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1})}e^{-i\mathbf{z}\cdot(\mathbf{k}_{2}-\mathbf{k}_{1})}\big(t^{c}U_{\mathbf{x}}U_{\mathbf{z}}^{\dagger}t^{c}U_{\mathbf{z}}-C_{F}\big)\;,

where it is understood that

\displaystyle\vec{\ell}_{3}

\displaystyle=\vec{k}_{2}-\vec{p}_{1}\;,\qquad\vec{\ell}_{2}=\vec{q}+\vec{p}_{1}-\vec{k}_{2}\;,

(444)

\displaystyle\vec{\ell}_{1}

\displaystyle=\vec{p}_{0}-\vec{k}_{1}\;,\qquad k^{+}=k_{1}^{+}=k_{2}^{+}\;.

Note that the Heaviside theta functions hidden in the phase-space
integrations of (
435
) result in upper and lower limits
for the integration over the gluon plus momentum.

The above expression can be simplified further by exploiting the properties
of the projectors
\mathcal{P}_{G,B}
, such as
u_{G}=\mathcal{P}_{G}u_{G}
,

\bar{u}_{G}\mathcal{P}_{B}=0
,
\gamma^{-}\mathcal{P}_{G}=\mathcal{P}_{G}\gamma^{+}=0
,
and the fact that they commute with the transverse gamma matrices:

[\mathcal{P}_{G,B},\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{i}]=0
. A generic spinor product then further simplifies into:

\displaystyle\bar{u}(\vec{k}_{1})\cancel{\epsilon}(\vec{k}_{3})u(\vec{k}_{2})\!=\!\bar{u}_{G}(k_{1}^{+})\Big[\gamma^{+}\epsilon^{-}(\vec{k}_{3})-\mathbf{k}_{1}\cdot\boldsymbol{\gamma}\frac{\gamma^{+}}{2k_{1}^{+}}\boldsymbol{\gamma}\cdot\boldsymbol{\epsilon}(\vec{k}_{3})-\boldsymbol{\gamma}\cdot\boldsymbol{\epsilon}(\vec{k}_{3})\frac{\gamma^{+}}{2k_{2}^{+}}\mathbf{k}_{2}\cdot\boldsymbol{\gamma}

(445)

\displaystyle+\!\mathbf{k}_{1}\cdot\boldsymbol{\gamma}\frac{\gamma^{+}}{2k_{1}^{+}}\gamma^{-}\frac{\gamma^{+}}{2k_{2}^{+}}\mathbf{k}_{2}\cdot\boldsymbol{\gamma}\epsilon^{+}(\vec{k}_{3})\Big]u_{G}(k_{2}^{+})\;,

\displaystyle=\!\bar{u}_{G}(k_{1}^{+})\gamma^{+}\Big[\!\epsilon^{-}(\vec{k}_{3})\!+\!\Big(\!\frac{\mathbf{k}_{1}^{i}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}^{j}(\vec{k}_{3})}{2k_{1}^{+}}\!+\!\frac{\boldsymbol{\epsilon}^{i}(\vec{k}_{3})\mathbf{k}_{2}^{j}}{2k_{2}^{+}}\!-\!\epsilon^{+}(\vec{k}_{3})\frac{\mathbf{k}_{1}^{i}\mathbf{k}_{2}^{j}}{2k_{1}^{+}k_{2}^{+}}\!\Big)\mathcal{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}^{i}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{j}\!\Big]u_{G}(k_{2}^{+})\;.

Finally, introducing
\sigma^{ij}=(i/2)[{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{i},{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{j}]
which
allows us to write
\mathcal{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}^{i}{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{j}=-\delta^{ij}-i\sigma^{ij}
,
we obtain the expression:

\displaystyle\bar{u}(\vec{k}_{1})\cancel{\epsilon}(\vec{k}_{3})u(\vec{k}_{2})

\displaystyle=\bar{u}_{G}(k_{1}^{+})\gamma^{+}\Bigg[\Big(\epsilon^{-}(\vec{k}_{3})-\frac{\mathbf{k}_{1}^{i}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}^{j}(\vec{k}_{3})}{2k_{1}^{+}}-\frac{\boldsymbol{\epsilon}^{i}(\vec{k}_{3})\mathbf{k}_{2}^{j}}{2k_{2}^{+}}+\epsilon^{+}(\vec{k}_{3})\frac{\mathbf{k}_{1}^{i}\mathbf{k}_{2}^{j}}{2k_{1}^{+}k_{2}^{+}}\Big)\delta^{ij}

(446)

\displaystyle-i\sigma^{ij}\Big(\frac{\mathbf{k}_{1}^{i}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}^{j}(\vec{k}_{3})}{2k_{1}^{+}}+\frac{\boldsymbol{\epsilon}^{i}(\vec{k}_{3})\mathbf{k}_{2}^{j}}{2k_{2}^{+}}-\epsilon^{+}(\vec{k}_{3})\frac{\mathbf{k}_{1}^{i}\mathbf{k}_{2}^{j}}{2k_{1}^{+}k_{2}^{+}}\Big)\Bigg]u_{G}(k_{2}^{+})\;.

With a suitable choice of polarization vectors, and taking the relation
between the different momenta into account, the above formula gives
rise to remarkably compact expressions. For instance, returning to
amplitude (
443
), we have the numerator:

\bar{u}(\vec{\ell}_{1}=\vec{p}_{0}-\vec{k}_{2})\cancel{\epsilon}_{\eta}^{*}(\vec{k}_{2})u(\vec{p}_{0})\;,

(447)

where
\eta=1,2
is the (transverse) polarization of the gluon. Choosing
the polarization vectors for the gluon to be linearly polarized:
\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{\eta}^{i}=\delta^{i\eta}
,
we have:

\displaystyle\epsilon_{\eta}^{\mu}(\vec{k})

\displaystyle=\big(0,\frac{\mathbf{k}\cdot\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{\eta}}{k^{+}},\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{\eta}\big)=\big(0,\frac{\mathbf{k}^{\eta}}{k^{+}},\delta^{i\eta}\big)\;,

(448)

applying (
446
) to the spinor product (
447
)
gives:

\displaystyle\bar{u}(\vec{\ell}_{1}\!=\!\vec{p}_{0}-\vec{k}_{1})\cancel{\epsilon}_{\eta}^{*}(\vec{k}_{1})u(\vec{p}_{0})

(449)

\displaystyle=\!\bar{u}_{G}(\ell_{1}^{+})\gamma^{+}\Big[\!\Big(\frac{\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\eta^{\prime}}}{k^{+}}\!-\!\frac{(\mathbf{p}_{0}-\mathbf{k}_{1})^{\eta^{\prime}}}{2(p_{0}^{+}\!-\!k^{+})}\!-\!\frac{\mathbf{p}_{0}^{\eta^{\prime}}}{2p_{0}^{+}}\Big)\delta^{\eta\eta^{\prime}}\!-\!i\sigma^{\eta\eta^{\prime}}\Big(\frac{\mathbf{p}_{0}^{\eta^{\prime}}}{2p_{0}^{+}}\!-\!\frac{(\mathbf{p}_{0}\!-\!\mathbf{k}_{1})^{\eta^{\prime}}}{2(p_{0}^{+}-k^{+})}\Big)\!\Big]u_{G}(p_{0}^{+})\;,

\displaystyle=\!\frac{p_{0}^{+}\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\eta^{\prime}}-k^{+}\mathbf{p}_{0}^{\eta^{\prime}}}{2p_{0}^{+}(p_{0}^{+}-k^{+})}\bar{u}_{G}(\ell_{1}^{+})\gamma^{+}\Big[\big(2\frac{p_{0}^{+}}{k^{+}}-1\big)\delta^{\eta\eta^{\prime}}-i\sigma^{\eta\eta^{\prime}}\Big]u_{G}(p_{0}^{+})\;,

\displaystyle=\!\frac{p_{0}^{+}\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\eta^{\prime}}-k^{+}\mathbf{p}_{0}^{\eta^{\prime}}}{2p_{0}^{+}(p_{0}^{+}-k^{+})}\bar{u}_{G}(\ell_{1}^{+})\gamma^{+}\mathrm{Dirac}^{\eta\eta^{\prime}}\big(2\frac{p_{0}^{+}}{k^{+}}-1\big)u_{G}(p_{0}^{+})\;.

Likewise:

\displaystyle\bar{u}(\vec{p}_{1})\cancel{\epsilon}_{\eta}(\vec{k}_{2})v(\vec{\ell}_{3})

\displaystyle=-\frac{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\bar{\eta}}-\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{\bar{\eta}}}{2(p_{1}^{+}-k^{+})}\bar{u}_{G}(p_{1}^{+})\gamma^{+}\mathrm{Dirac}^{\eta\bar{\eta}}\Big(1-\frac{2p_{1}^{+}}{k^{+}}\Big)v_{G}(\ell_{3}^{+})\;,

(450)

where, for later convenience, we introduced the momentum combination:

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\ell}

\displaystyle\equiv\mathbf{k}_{2}-\frac{k^{+}}{p_{0}^{+}}\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\;.

(451)

The last spinor product in (
443
) involves the external virtual photon. When the latter is transversely polarized, we obtain a similar expression to the ones with a gluon:

\displaystyle\bar{v}_{G}(\vec{\ell}_{3})\cancel{\epsilon}_{\lambda}^{*}(\vec{q})u_{G}(\vec{\ell}_{2})

\displaystyle=\frac{q^{+}\Big(\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\bar{\lambda}}-\frac{p_{0}^{+}-k^{+}}{q^{+}}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{\bar{\lambda}}\Big)}{2(p_{1}^{+}-k^{+})(p_{0}^{+}-k^{+})}

(452)

\displaystyle\times\bar{v}_{G}(\ell_{3}^{+})\gamma^{+}\mathrm{Dirac}^{\lambda\bar{\lambda}}\Big(1+\frac{2(p_{1}^{+}-k^{+})}{q^{+}}\Big)u_{G}(\ell_{2}^{+})\;.

However, in the longitudinal case, the spinor product looks quite
different:

\displaystyle\bar{v}_{G}(\vec{\ell}_{3})\cancel{\epsilon}_{0}^{*}(\vec{q})u_{G}(\vec{\ell}_{2})

\displaystyle=\!\frac{q^{+}}{2(p_{1}^{+}\!-\!k^{+})(p_{0}^{+}\!-\!k^{+})M}

(453)

\displaystyle\times\Big[\!\Big(\boldsymbol{\ell}\!-\!\frac{p_{0}^{+}-k^{+}}{q^{+}}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}\!\Big)^{2}\!-\!\hat{M}^{2}\!\Big]\bar{v}_{G}(\ell_{3}^{+})\gamma^{+}u_{G}(\ell_{2}^{+})\;,

where
\hat{M}^{2}
as defined in (
75
).

Finally, it is convenient to cast the energy denominators in a form dictated
by the momentum structure of the spinor products (
449
),
(
450
), and (
452
), yielding after some
algebra:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p_{0}^{-}-k_{1}^{-}-\ell_{1}^{-}}

\displaystyle=\frac{-2k^{+}(p_{0}^{+}-k^{+})}{p_{0}^{+}\mathbf{k}_{1}^{2}}\;,

(454)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{q^{-}-\ell_{2}^{-}-\ell_{3}^{-}}

\displaystyle=\frac{2(p_{1}^{+}-k^{+})(p_{0}^{+}-k^{+})}{q^{+}}\frac{1}{\Big(\boldsymbol{\ell}-\frac{p_{0}^{+}-k^{+}}{q^{+}}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}\Big)^{2}+\hat{M}^{2}}\;,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{q^{-}+p_{1}^{-}-\ell_{2}^{-}-k_{2}^{-}}

\displaystyle=\frac{-2k^{+}(p_{0}^{+}-k^{+})}{p_{0}^{+}}\frac{1}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}+\Delta_{\mathrm{P}}}\;,

where
\Delta_{\mathrm{P}}
was defined in equation (
52
).

Gathering our expressions (
449
), (
450
),
(
452
), and (
453
) for the numerators,
together with the calculation (
454
) of the denominators,
using (
444
) and (
451
) to rewrite the
phases, we finally obtain:

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{A2}}^{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{M}\int_{p_{1}^{+}}^{p_{0}^{+}}\frac{\mathrm{d}k^{+}}{2\pi 2k^{+}}\frac{(k^{+})^{2}(p_{0}^{+}-k^{+})}{(p_{0}^{+})^{2}(p_{1}^{+}-k^{+})}\mathrm{Dirac}_{\mathrm{V}}^{\bar{\eta}0\eta^{\prime}}

(455)

\displaystyle\times\int_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{z}}\int_{\mathbf{k}_{1}}\frac{\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\eta^{\prime}}}{\mathbf{k}_{1}^{2}}e^{-i\mathbf{k}_{1}\cdot(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{z})}\int_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}e^{-i\boldsymbol{\ell}\cdot(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{z})}\frac{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\bar{\eta}}+\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{\bar{\eta}}}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}+\Delta_{\mathrm{P}}}\frac{\Big(\boldsymbol{\ell}+\frac{p_{0}^{+}-k^{+}}{q^{+}}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}\Big)^{2}-\hat{M}^{2}}{\Big(\boldsymbol{\ell}+\frac{p_{0}^{+}-k^{+}}{q^{+}}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}\Big)^{2}+\hat{M}^{2}}

\displaystyle\times e^{-i\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\Big(\frac{p_{0}^{+}-k^{+}}{p_{0}^{+}}\mathbf{x}+\frac{k^{+}}{p_{0}^{+}}\mathbf{z}\Big)}\big(t^{c}U_{\mathbf{x}}U_{\mathbf{z}}^{\dagger}t^{c}U_{\mathbf{z}}-C_{F}\big)\;,

for the longitudinal polarization, and

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{A2}}^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=-\int_{p_{1}^{+}}^{p_{0}^{+}}\frac{\mathrm{d}k^{+}}{2\pi 2k^{+}}\frac{(k^{+})^{2}(p_{0}^{+}-k^{+})}{(p_{0}^{+})^{2}(p_{1}^{+}-k^{+})}\mathrm{Dirac}_{\mathrm{V}}^{\bar{\eta}\bar{\lambda}\eta^{\prime}}

(456)

\displaystyle\times\int_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{z}}\int_{\mathbf{k}_{1}}\frac{\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\eta^{\prime}}}{\mathbf{k}_{1}^{2}}e^{-i\mathbf{k}_{1}\cdot(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{z})}\int_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}e^{-i\boldsymbol{\ell}\cdot(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{z})}\frac{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\bar{\eta}}+\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{\bar{\eta}}}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}+\Delta_{\mathrm{P}}}\frac{\Big(\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\bar{\lambda}}+\frac{p_{0}^{+}-k^{+}}{q^{+}}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{\bar{\lambda}}\Big)}{\Big(\boldsymbol{\ell}+\frac{p_{0}^{+}-k^{+}}{q^{+}}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}\Big)^{2}+\hat{M}^{2}}

\displaystyle\times e^{-i\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\Big(\frac{p_{0}^{+}-k^{+}}{p_{0}^{+}}\mathbf{x}+\frac{k^{+}}{p_{0}^{+}}\mathbf{z}\Big)}\big(t^{c}U_{\mathbf{x}}U_{\mathbf{z}}^{\dagger}t^{c}U_{\mathbf{z}}-C_{F}\big)\;,

in the transverse case.

Appendix D
Ultraviolet behavior of amplitude
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{V2}}

To investigate whether the transverse integral in (
87
)
exhibits an UV divergence in the
\mathbf{z}\to\mathbf{x}
limit,
we set
\mathbf{z}=\mathbf{x}
in the last line, after which
\mathbf{z}

can be integrated over in the second line. We obtain:

\displaystyle\lim_{\mathbf{z}\to\mathbf{x}}\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathrm{V2}}^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\alpha_{s}C_{F}\int_{k_{\mathrm{min}}^{+}}^{p_{1}^{+}}\frac{\mathrm{d}k^{+}}{k^{+}}\frac{(k^{+})^{3}q^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}(p_{0}^{+})^{2}(p_{1}^{+}-k^{+})}\mathrm{Dirac}_{\mathrm{V}}^{\bar{\eta}\bar{\lambda}\eta^{\prime}}

(457)

\displaystyle\times\int_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}\frac{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\eta^{\prime}}}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}}\frac{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\bar{\eta}}-\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{\bar{\eta}}}{\big(\boldsymbol{\ell}-\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}\big)^{2}}\frac{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\bar{\lambda}}-\frac{p_{0}^{+}-k^{+}}{q^{+}}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{\bar{\lambda}}}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}+\Delta_{\mathrm{P}}}

\displaystyle\times\int_{\mathbf{x}}e^{-i\mathbf{k}_{\perp}\cdot\mathbf{x}}[U_{\mathbf{x}}-1]\;.

The integral over
\boldsymbol{\ell}
, which we denote
\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{V2}}^{\eta^{\prime}\bar{\eta}\lambda}
,
contains three open transverse indices. However, when multiplying
(
83
) with
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LO}}^{\lambda^{\prime}\dagger}

to obtain the cross section, the trace of the Dirac structures
\mathrm{Dirac}_{\mathrm{LO}}^{\lambda^{\prime}\dagger}\mathrm{Dirac}_{\mathrm{V}}^{\bar{\eta}\bar{\lambda}\eta^{\prime}}
will
yield only the Lorentz structures
\delta^{\bar{\eta}\eta^{\prime}}

and
\epsilon^{\bar{\eta}\eta^{\prime}}
. We will use this knowledge
and only calculate the projections
\delta^{\bar{\eta}\eta^{\prime}}\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{V2}}^{\eta^{\prime}\bar{\eta}\lambda}

and
\epsilon^{\bar{\eta}\eta^{\prime}}\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{V2}}^{\eta^{\prime}\bar{\eta}\lambda}
,
dramatically simplifying the evaluation. Using the identities (
408
)
and (
413
), one finds:

\displaystyle\delta^{\bar{\eta}\eta^{\prime}}\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{V2}}^{\eta^{\prime}\bar{\eta}\lambda}

\displaystyle=\int_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}\frac{1}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}}\frac{\boldsymbol{\ell}\cdot\big(\boldsymbol{\ell}-\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}\big)}{\big(\boldsymbol{\ell}-\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}\big)^{2}}\frac{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\bar{\lambda}}-\frac{p_{0}^{+}-k^{+}}{q^{+}}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{\bar{\lambda}}}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}+\Delta_{\mathrm{P}}}\;,

(458)

\displaystyle=\frac{k^{+}}{2p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{\bar{\lambda}}\mathcal{B}_{1}\big(0,\Delta_{\mathrm{P}},\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}\big)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\Big(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{p_{0}^{+}(p_{1}^{+}-k^{+})}{k^{+}q^{+}}\Big)\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{\bar{\lambda}}

\displaystyle\times\Bigg[\mathcal{B}_{0}(\Delta_{\mathrm{P}})+\mathcal{B}_{0}\big(\Delta_{\mathrm{P}},\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}\big)-\Big(\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\Big)^{2}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{2}\mathcal{C}_{0}\big(\Delta_{\mathrm{P}},\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}\big)\Bigg]\;,

and

\displaystyle\epsilon^{\bar{\eta}\eta^{\prime}}\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{V2}}^{\eta^{\prime}\bar{\eta}\lambda}

\displaystyle=\epsilon^{\bar{\eta}\eta^{\prime}}\int_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}\frac{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\eta^{\prime}}}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}}\frac{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\bar{\eta}}-\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{\bar{\eta}}}{\big(\boldsymbol{\ell}-\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}\big)^{2}}\frac{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\bar{\lambda}}-\frac{p_{0}^{+}-k^{+}}{q^{+}}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{\bar{\lambda}}}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}+\Delta_{\mathrm{P}}}

(459)

\displaystyle=\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{\bar{\eta}}\epsilon^{\bar{\eta}\bar{\lambda}}\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{D-3}\Bigg[\mathcal{B}_{1}\big(0,\Delta_{\mathrm{P}},\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}\big)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{B}_{0}(\Delta_{\mathrm{P}})-\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{B}_{0}\big(\Delta_{\mathrm{P}},\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}\big)+\frac{1}{2}\Big(\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\Big)^{2}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}^{2}\mathcal{C}_{0}\big(\Delta_{\mathrm{P}},\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}\big)\Bigg]\;.

Neither of the above results contain an UV divergence. Moreover, the
infrared poles contained in the structures
\mathcal{B}_{0}(\Delta_{\mathrm{P}})
,

\mathcal{B}_{0}\big(\Delta_{\mathrm{P}},\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}\big)
,
and
\mathcal{C}_{0}\big(\Delta_{\mathrm{P}},\frac{k^{+}}{p_{1}^{+}}\mathbf{P}_{\perp}\big)

all cancel (see eq.(
416
)). Expressions (
458
)
and (
459
) are, therefore, finite.

Appendix E
Quark field-strength renormalization

We consider a dressed quark Fock state, and expand it perturbatively
to first order:

\displaystyle|\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{0})\rangle

\displaystyle=\!\sqrt{\mathcal{Z}}|\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{0})\rangle_{0}+\int\mathrm{PS}(\vec{p}_{1},\vec{k})\frac{{}_{0}\langle q(\vec{p}_{1}),g(\vec{k})|\hat{V}|q(\vec{p}_{0})\rangle_{0}}{p_{0}^{-}\!-\!k^{-}\!-\!p_{1}^{-}}|\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{1}),\mathbf{g}(\vec{k})\rangle_{0}\!+\!\mathcal{O}(g_{s}^{2})\;.

(460)

The interaction Hamiltonian sandwiched between the bare Fock states
yields:

\displaystyle{}_{0}\langle\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{1}),\mathbf{g}(\vec{k})|\hat{V}|\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{0})\rangle_{0}

\displaystyle=(2\pi)^{D-1}\delta^{(D-1)}\big(\vec{p}_{0}-\vec{k}-\vec{p}_{1}\big)g_{s}t^{c}\bar{u}(\vec{p}_{1})\cancel{\epsilon}_{\eta}^{*}(\vec{k})u(\vec{p}_{0})\;,

(461)

\displaystyle=(2\pi)^{D-1}\delta^{(D-1)}\big(\vec{p}_{0}-\vec{k}-\vec{p}_{1}\big)g_{s}t^{c}

\displaystyle\times\frac{\big(\mathbf{k}-\frac{k^{+}}{p_{0}^{+}}\mathbf{p}_{0}\big)^{\bar{\eta}}}{2(p_{0}^{+}-k^{+})}\mathrm{Dirac}^{\eta\bar{\eta}}\Big(2\frac{p_{0}^{+}}{k^{+}}-1\Big)\;,

while the energy denominator gives:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p_{0}^{-}-k^{-}-p_{1}^{-}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{2k^{+}(p_{0}^{+}-k^{+})}{p_{0}^{+}\big(\mathbf{k}-\frac{k^{+}}{p_{0}^{+}}\mathbf{p}_{0}\big)^{2}}\;.

(462)

Combining the above results, we obtain the following result for the
first-order expansion of the dressed quark state, temporarily indicating all the spin- and color indices

\displaystyle|\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{0},s,i)\rangle

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\mathcal{Z}}|\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{0},s,i)\rangle_{0}

(463)

\displaystyle-\sum_{j,\tilde{s},h,c}\int\mathrm{PS}(\vec{p}_{1},\vec{k})(2\pi)^{D-1}\delta^{(D-1)}\big(\vec{p}_{0}-\vec{k}-\vec{p}_{1}\big)

\displaystyle\times g_{s}t_{ij}^{c}\frac{k^{+}}{p_{0}^{+}}\frac{\big(\mathbf{k}-\frac{k^{+}}{p_{0}^{+}}\mathbf{p}_{0}\big)^{\bar{\eta}}}{\big(\mathbf{k}-\frac{k^{+}}{p_{0}^{+}}\mathbf{p}_{0}\big)^{2}}\mathrm{Dirac}^{\eta\bar{\eta}}\Big(2\frac{p_{0}^{+}}{k^{+}}-1\Big)|\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{1},\tilde{s},j),\mathbf{g}(\vec{k},h,c)\rangle_{0}.

Per definition, we require the Fock states to be normalized in the
sense of a distribution:

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{0}^{\prime},s^{\prime},i^{\prime})|\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{0},s,i)\rangle

\displaystyle=2p_{0}^{+}\delta_{s^{\prime}s}\delta_{i^{\prime}i}(2\pi)^{D-1}\delta^{(D-1)}\big(\vec{p}_{0}-\vec{p}_{0}^{\prime}\big)\;,

(464)

or:

\frac{1}{2N_{c}}\sum_{s^{\prime}i^{\prime}}\begin{aligned} \int\mathrm{PS}(\vec{p}_{0}^{\prime}){}_{0}\langle\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{0}^{\prime},s^{\prime},i^{\prime})|\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{0},s,i)\rangle&=1\;.\end{aligned}

(465)

Since the above is supposed to hold to any order of perturbation theory,
we can write:

\displaystyle 1

\displaystyle=\mathcal{Z}+\frac{1}{2N_{c}}\int\mathrm{PS}(\vec{p}_{0}^{\prime},\vec{p}_{1},\vec{k},\vec{p}_{1}^{\prime},\vec{k}^{\prime})(2\pi)^{D-1}\delta^{(D-1)}\big(\vec{p}_{0}-\vec{k}-\vec{p}_{1}\big)

(466)

\displaystyle\times(2\pi)^{D-1}\delta^{(D-1)}\big(\vec{p}_{0}^{\prime}-\vec{k}^{\prime}-\vec{p}_{1}^{\prime}\big)g_{s}^{2}\mathrm{Tr}(t^{c}t^{c})\frac{k^{+}}{p_{0}^{+}}\frac{k^{\prime+}}{p_{0}^{\prime+}}

\displaystyle\times\frac{\big(\mathbf{k}-\frac{k^{+}}{p_{0}^{+}}\mathbf{p}_{0}\big)^{\bar{\eta}}}{\big(\mathbf{k}-\frac{k^{+}}{p_{0}^{+}}\mathbf{p}_{0}\big)^{2}}\frac{\big(\mathbf{k}^{\prime}-\frac{k^{\prime+}}{p_{0}^{\prime+}}\mathbf{p}_{0}^{\prime}\big)^{\eta^{\prime}}}{\big(\mathbf{k}^{\prime}-\frac{k^{\prime+}}{p_{0}^{\prime+}}\mathbf{p}_{0}^{\prime}\big)^{2}}\mathrm{Dirac}^{\eta\eta^{\prime}\dagger}\Big(2\frac{p_{0}^{\prime+}}{k^{\prime+}}-1\Big)\mathrm{Dirac}^{\eta\bar{\eta}}\Big(2\frac{p_{0}^{+}}{k^{+}}-1\Big)

\displaystyle\times{}_{0}\langle\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{1}^{\prime}),\mathbf{g}(\vec{k}^{\prime})|\mathbf{q}(\vec{p}_{1}),\mathbf{g}(\vec{k})\rangle_{0}\;,

\displaystyle=\mathcal{Z}+\alpha_{s}C_{F}\int_{\mathbf{k}}\frac{1}{\mathbf{k}^{2}}\times\int_{k_{\mathrm{min}}^{+}}^{p_{0}^{+}}\frac{\mathrm{d}k^{+}}{k^{+}}\frac{(k^{+})^{2}}{(p_{0}^{+})^{2}}\Big[\big(1-\frac{2p_{0}^{+}}{k^{+}}\big)^{2}+(D-3)\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{Z}+\alpha_{s}C_{F}\int_{\mathbf{k}}\frac{1}{\mathbf{k}^{2}}\times\Big[-3+4\mathrm{ln}\frac{p_{0}^{+}}{k_{\mathrm{min}}^{+}}\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{Z}+\frac{\alpha_{s}C_{F}}{2\pi}\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{UV}}}-\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{IR}}}\Big)\Big(-\frac{3}{2}+2\mathrm{ln}\frac{p_{0}^{+}}{k_{\mathrm{min}}^{+}}\Big)+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon_{\mathrm{UV}})\;,

and finally:

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}

\displaystyle=\Bigg[1-\frac{\alpha_{s}C_{F}}{2\pi}\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{UV}}}-\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{IR}}}\Big)\Big(-3+2\mathrm{ln}\frac{p_{0}^{+}}{k_{\mathrm{min}}^{+}}+2\mathrm{ln}\frac{p_{1}^{+}}{k_{\mathrm{min}}^{+}}\Big)\Bigg]\;.

(467)
